# Acceptance contract

Version 3, 2026-09-24 (version 2 2026-09-05; version 1 earlier that day; section 23 and the amendments listed under Changes were added at 13:50 EDT on 2026-09-05; sections 24 to 30 on 2026-09-24). This document binds milestones 1 to 3 of `plan.md` and milestone 9, the desktop workspace (sections 24 to 30); sections 23 to 30 name the milestone each clause is first tested in. Code is built against it; tests cite its clauses by number (for example `C-5.4`). A clause changes only by editing this file in the same commit as the code and tests that depend on it. Where this file and `plan.md` disagree, this file wins.

Language: Python 3.12 or newer, standard library only. Packaging with `uv`. No bash runners; the one bash file is the never-rules guard hook, copied byte for byte from v1.

## Changes in version 3

- C-11.10 (2026-10-29, #260 review r6, R6-1): distinguish a release's instant from an ending by the clock. The clock ending reads the job row's `next_check_at` inside its transaction and ends there, or at now if that clock is still ahead; a release ends at its own instant, or at the row's clock if that passed unseen. Each ending is still applied at most once. The report's invariant 1 states the same rule.
- C-26.4 (2026-10-29, D-RELAY-EXIT1 fix r4, #271): the relay learns of its child's exit from the kernel without reaping it. The guardian's blocking wait holds Popen's lock, and `poll()` then reports no exit even for a child that has exited (review r3's P2-1), so the relay's status, a refusal's `child_exited` flag and signal delivery read Popen's own record or else peek with `waitid(WEXITED | WNOHANG | WNOWAIT)`: through `os.waitid`, or libc's `waitid` by ctypes where CPython leaves it out (3.12 on macOS). ECHILD means the guardian's wait reaped the child; any other failure proves nothing. No peek reaps; whichever of the guardian's wait and Popen's `poll()` reaps the child, Popen stores its real status.
- C-26.4 (2026-10-27 and 2026-10-28, D-RELAY-EXIT1 reviews r1 to r3, #271): keep proved exit evidence even if `exit.json` never arrives, including a fresh `status` naming the child `exited`, which proves the exit whatever the log shows, so a runner rebuilt after a restart proves it again. Drain stdout to EOF. Every chunk on disk when the drain is entered, by any path (a proved exit, the receipt or an owner-ended attempt), is read; output written after that is bounded by `EXIT_DRAIN_S` (30 s from the read that catches up with that size; each rebuild can add one bound), checked after each chunk, then request containment and settle from the output already read. Relay I/O ending is permanent and independent of `relay_failed`; every later frame path, including initialize replay, steers, title requests and stdin close, stays closed. Review r3: a failed stat of stdout at the drain's entry is tried again, and a size still unknown is never zero (the first read that reaches the end of the file catches up); exit proof counts when a loss is classified, so a later receipt does not undo `relay-failed`; and a runner that ends on an unexpected exception settles its turn as an unproved loss, unless its service is closing.

- C-26.4 (2026-10-27, D-RELAY-EXIT1): distinguish a refused frame after a proved child exit from failure while the child lives. Refusals can carry the relay's reaped-child evidence, surviving socket shutdown before `exit.json`; older replies can be checked against status or the receipt. The provider's result or EOF decides an exited child's turn, including on replay.

- C-27.5 (2026-10-27, D-QT-FLAKE1 round 11, review r9): distinguish the own-send landing check from following in the normative clause. A quiet measured landing ends the check and emits its callback once; following and document-growth re-pinning continue until the person's scroll, a new timeline reveal or the view's removal. The own-send exception to marker visibility has that same following lifetime. This clarifies round 10's behavior; its change-log entry now uses the same distinction. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-27-qt-flake-r11.md`.
- C-27.5, C-29.12 (2026-10-26, D-QT-FLAKE1 rounds 8–10, reviews r6–r8): a reveal whose target row leaves the model before it is presented, or is not in the model when it is requested, is abandoned at once; the view cancels that request and releases suspension if the end marker is on screen, and otherwise the marker's next appearance releases it (an event-log reset had removed a pending reveal's row and left the view 1,050 pt behind the next reply). The person's own send ends any reveal and releases suspension during catch-up too, where it had chosen `stay` (2,232 pt behind, 18,529 pt after catch-up and a reply); the landing check continues until the measured end settles (a glide stopped 208 pt short of a document that then grew 224 pt, leaving a reply 610 pt behind), while following continues until the person scrolls, a new timeline reveal starts or the view is removed, settled or not, and document growth is re-pinned to the end (600 pt of growth after a quiet landing had left a reply 779 pt behind). The opening pin checks a reveal's row whenever its place in the document changes, not only when its own frame does. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-14-queue-tray.md`, rounds 8–10.
- C-27.5, C-29.12 (2026-10-25, D-QT-FLAKE1 round 7, CI run 40251214771): a timeline reveal is outstanding from its request until its own row is presented in the clip, and while it is outstanding an end that comes into view releases nothing: lazy measurement had shrunk the document under a clip the reveal had just moved, the clip clamped to the end, and the marker and the request's completion both released suspension before the row was shown. The row's presentation with the end beside it releases suspension; after it, the marker appearing does. The person's own scroll or send ends an outstanding reveal, and the end then in view releases suspension. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-14-queue-tray.md`, round 7.
- C-27.5, C-29.7, C-29.12 (2026-10-24, D-QT-FLAKE1, rounds 3–4): explicit timeline reveals suspend settling and ordinary following while the lazy end marker is stale. Own sends still glide during ordinary following. The marker appearing or a matching completed scroll confirming the end remains visible releases suspension without scrolling; catch-up resumes on the next changed key. Tray-only reveals leave following alone.

- C-4.2, C-5.6, C-5.7 (2026-10-28, review r3 of #250's P3-1, P3-2, check 5; #276; `docs/reports/2026-10-20-kill4-terminal-containment.md`): C-5.7: a plain `kill` on a finished job whose census is unverifiable and has no member C-5.4 lets the protocol signal runs the protocol only when a verifiable read could attribute a member, or the census has none (the listing failed), and otherwise answers `already finished`, naming the members `held, never signalled`. In case 3's world (a stranger's leaderless group at the recorded pgid, with a legacy attempt boot or an unreadable current boot) every census is unverifiable, and each `kill` had run a protocol that could signal nothing and never reported the last result. Review r1 of #276 (P2-1): the first form of the rule counted only walk and responsibility members, so a finished job whose escaped, marked runner had no start in the census's process table answered `already finished` and left that runner running, where the rule before it had run the protocol and TERMed it; a marked or parent-chain member, a member of the recorded group under a verified leader, or an owned member (review r2 of #276), whose identity the census could not read now counts too, and a member only a leaderless group at the recorded pgid names still does not. Review r3 of #276 (P3-1): an owned record counts only at a pid the census has not proven recycled, so a stranger's member at a pid an inspection once recorded as owned for an earlier process no longer runs, with the boot unreadable, a protocol on every `kill` that could signal nothing. C-5.6: a `starting` attempt whose last read of start.json, taken after its abandon, finds none has its group KILLed at once with no TERM, at an expired start grace and at a cancel or a wall stop while it is starting; "need none" now covers only a `reserved` attempt. The code already worked so. C-4.2: the first read that finds an extended attempt's start.json records `attempt.start_landed` once (`seconds` from the deadline's start, `deadline_start`, and the grace and bound in force), the 60 s bound's production record; it decides nothing.
- C-12.4, C-26.11 (2026-10-28, D-WATCHDOG1, d1031; `docs/reports/2026-10-28-lane-retry-watchdog.md`): every Claude launch that starts a model turn passes exactly one `--settings`, whose `env` sets `CLAUDE_CODE_RETRY_WATCHDOG` to `0`: detached and resumed jobs, admission probes, keepalive and heal timer turns, gate rounds, conversation turns of every permission, enrolment and probe turns, and the card heal. Max's d1031 allows the watchdog for travel only once the lanes do not inherit it. A lane has no home of its own, so the user's `settings.json` `env` reached every launch that loads user settings, and under the watchdog a lane at its plan's ceiling retries 429 and 529 responses without end instead of exiting, past C-4.5's `limited` failover until the wall. The publish hook and a turn's `disableAllHooks`, `fastMode` and `ultracode` keep their places in that one payload, and a read-only fast turn's `fastMode` joins it rather than keeping a flag of its own.
- C-23.14 (2026-10-28, D-SCR2 fix round 10, #239 review r9b; `docs/reports/2026-10-18-scrub-inline-headers.md`, "Fix round 10"): every bounded mode now writes its text kept for the head's runs before it cuts it, as round 9 made `clean` within the budget do. Past the budget, `scrub_bounded` and `clean` had cut release's excerpts to the limit and then kept each for the runs of the head's scrub of the whole excerpt, which holds no part-word, so each cut's part-word became `[REDACTED]`, the result passed the limit, and `truncate` cut it again through release's marker: 300,000 characters of prose bounded to 4,000 wrote `… [6 characters omitted] …` beside `ACTED]`, where release writes 296,034 (P2-1). `scrubbed_line` had cut release's line first, so the part-word at its cut became `[REDACTED]` and an ordinary word was lost (`directory: [REDACTED]…`, and seventy `x`s at 60 became `[REDACTED]…`, P2-2). Each excerpt is now kept so, then cut as release cuts its own, with release's marker, the count of what release's own cut of the same excerpts leaves out; the pieces fit the limit, so nothing cuts them again; and `scrubbed_line` is `bounded(shown_by_both(released, head), limit, mark)`. The trade is round 9's: a cut can keep a character release's own cut left out, but never one either scrub hides. The clause now says the excerpts are cut where both scrubbers allow a cut (it said where release cuts, false wherever the head refuses a break release allows, P3-3), and that the head's hiding holds per value, not per place (P3-1).
- C-23.14, C-23.36 (2026-10-28, D-SCR2 fix round 9, CI run 40545353102 at `67674bb14`; `docs/reports/2026-10-18-scrub-inline-headers.md`, "Fix round 9"): within the budget, `scrub_bounded` and `clean` are again exactly the whole-text scrub cut by `truncate` (`test_within_the_budget_it_is_scrub_then_truncate`, and CI's case pinned by name). Round 8 had cut release's scrub into `truncate`'s pieces and kept each for the runs the head's scrub of the whole text holds, but a cut through a run leaves a piece the head's scrub holds only whole, so `password = ` three times, bounded to 30, became `[`, 9 characters omitted, `=` where C-23.36 and release write `p`, 6 omitted, `=` (1,866 of 5,000 random cases of the property differed). The bound now cuts the composed scrub where `truncate` cuts any text, as release cuts its own scrub, so every character it keeps is one the whole-text mode writes; its cut can fall elsewhere than release's cut of its own output, which the clause's "no mode shows a character that release/217's same mode hides" did not allow for, so it now says what the scrub takes out and names the cut. A longer text's excerpts are unchanged, and the excerpt property now also places values only the head's scrub hides. No text of review r8's corpora (461,248, the longest scrubbing to 635 characters) reaches the changed branch at `clean`'s 4,000, so round 8's counts there stand.
- C-23.14, C-25.7, C-27.1 (2026-10-27, D-SCR2 fix round 8, #239 review r8; `docs/reports/2026-10-18-scrub-inline-headers.md`, "Fix round 8"): every mode now runs release/217's pipeline for that mode, frozen in `subfleet/sessions/scrub_release.py` (release/217 at `2c981cba5`, verbatim but for its imports, pinned by `test_scrub_release_frozen`), and the head's pipeline for it, both over the same text, and writes release's output but for each whitespace-free run of it that the head's output does not hold, which becomes `[REDACTED]`; approvals mask what release's masking leaves. So "the head shows nothing release hides" holds by construction, in every mode, where rounds 5 to 8 each closed the families their review found and the next review's generators found more (review r8: 598 of 146,793 new texts, four families; its nine inputs, `test_scrub_review_r8`, pass). The hub's wording composed the head's pipeline on release's output; built that way it showed nothing release hides but lost the head's gains in 91,978 of the 461,248 corpus texts, since release's rule takes an item whose value is past what it redacts (`token:--SECRET7…_token SECRET7…`) and the head's pass then cannot see the item, so the head reads the text instead and its output decides only what more of release's to hide. Over review r8's corpora table (461,248 texts, review r7's eight sets among them) no mode shows a value release's same mode hides, none shows one `be4f090b6` hid, no excerpt cut where both scrubbers allow shows a value the whole hides, and the head hides what release shows in 130,859 texts (130,712 at `be4f090b6`). A longer text's excerpts are cut where both scrubbers allow a cut: release's own cuts let an excerpt show a value its whole text hides, past a break the head refuses. The clause's rules are the head's pipeline and its examples that pipeline's output, which a mode writes where release's output holds the same runs: a run either hides goes whole, its punctuation with it (`curl -H 'Authorization: Basic SECRET7…' https://x` is written `curl -H 'Authorization: [REDACTED] [REDACTED] https://x`). Review r8's check 6: the converse sentence no longer calls `SECRET7_token` the one value a reference hides that this scrubber shows (3fbc4b7c5 is no reference, and release's own pipeline shows it); round 7's entry, round 6's and the report's round-7 invariant 1 carry dated corrections; the memo sentence says the count test covers the unclosed values only. The `Bearer` sentence says each mode also runs release's own lookahead, so a scrub costs release's work and the head's (the tests' cost bound against release's scrub is 2.5).
- C-23.14 (2026-10-26, D-SCR2 fix round 7, #239 review r7; `docs/reports/2026-10-18-scrub-inline-headers.md`, "Fix round 7"): round 6's rule now holds at the quoted pass's two other places where a credential flag can end a value: the value of a header's name that a quoted match swallows, read to its line's end, and a key's plain value within a quoted match. Such a flag, whose name is itself a key whose separator is on the next line, ends nothing there either, and the quoted value takes it with that key's value, as both references do in the excerpt, whole and `clean` modes (corrected 2026-10-27, review r8 P2-1: not in daemon.log's two lines, where the references' fold gives such a flag its whole run past a key whose next-line value is shorter, `:=,SECRET7…`). That closes review r7's P2-1 family (`Authorization:"Authorization:`, `--password` and `:r SECRET7…` on three lines; `Set-Cookie:"Authorization`, `:--1_token` and `=r SECRET7…`), which showed the last line's word in the excerpt, whole and `clean` modes where release be0c2a311 and both references hide it, in every round's head since 8ed254385 (review r7: 5,095 of its 51,371 family texts and 2 of its 15,000 joins at 36590532c; none now). Round 6's entry said the converse was again the one value a reference hides that this scrubber is known to show; that was false while the family showed, and it held on every text of review r7's eight corpora (311,789), the only texts this round ran (corrected 2026-10-27: review r8's generators found 598 more texts in four families, P2-1 to P2-4, that the head showed and release hid, so it was not true past them; round 8 makes it hold by construction): the head shows none that 473fe2941, c2e080e44 or release hides, and on the regression inputs, the joins and the family none that 3fbc4b7c5 hides but the converse. So the converse sentence names the report's round-7 section with the rounds whose generators it counts, and round 6's entry says its claim held only from this round. The `keyed` memo's sentence (the unclosed values on a line read its end once) is unchanged; a test now counts it.
- C-23.14 (2026-10-26, D-SCR2 fix round 6, #239 review r6; `docs/reports/2026-10-18-scrub-inline-headers.md`, "Fix round 6"): a credential flag that ends a quoted value with no closing quote, and whose name is itself a key whose separator is on the next line, ends nothing: the quoted value takes the flag with that key's value, as both references do. That closes round 5's stated P3-4 join and its ordinary-flag form (`Authorization:"--password` and `=r SECRET7…` on the next line), which showed the word after the run in up to all five modes where release be0c2a311 and both references hide it (review r6, P2-1: 3,693 of its 345,959 texts; 2,860 of this round's 48,758 at 1ad7da99c, none now). So the stated exception is removed, and the converse is again the one value a reference hides that this scrubber is known to show (not so in any round: round 7's entry claimed it from round 7, and review r8 found four more families; corrected 2026-10-27, round 8 replaces the claim with the composition's guarantee). The lone-flag sentence names two cases it does not reach, which show the value in the excerpt, whole and `clean` modes as both references do (review r6, P3-1): a quoted header on the line after the header, and a header there that isn't at the value's start; round 4's entry no longer says "now true", nor round 5's that it closed the rest. The folded-line trade the clause states, restated per corpus: daemon.log's line or `detail_line` shows a value the head before hid in 3 of round 5's 243,680 texts (review r5 counted 27 in its own), in 763 of review r6's 53,825 line-start texts against be6fb4330 without its skip, and, with this round's change, in 389 of this round's 48,758 (365 of the P3-4 class's 33,740 texts, 24 of 15,000 joins). All are in those two line modes only, and each is a value both references show there.
- C-23.14 (2026-10-26, D-SCR2 fix round 5, #239 review r5; `docs/reports/2026-10-18-scrub-inline-headers.md`, "Fix round 5"): a header that the line-start rule reads across a line break leaves no value to an earlier rule. A fold, a refused `Bearer`'s line and the run the `Bearer` rule redacts before a key's credential word take nothing where that header's value starts on a later line than their run (`Set-Cookie :` and `;v` on the next line), and a header, inline or at a line start, whose value would start on the next line leaves that line's own header to it, one the line-start rule reads there included (`Set-Cookie` and `:v` on the next line); the inline next-line sentence and the fold sentence were false (review r5, P2-1: 404 of its 114,946 generated texts showed a value both references hide). A refused `Bearer`'s line whose run is a header's name takes nothing where the line would end early, as a fold's does; on any other line it ends before the item as before (review r5's rule, applied to every such line, showed a word no rule owns that 8fde0c4ad hid: `Authorization: Bearer` and `client_secret=x SECRET7… --password`). So the lone-flag sentence holds for a fold, a refused `Bearer`'s line and a header whose value would start on that line, which it now names, but for two cases review r6 found (round 6 names them). The prefix rule keeps a name's leading `-`, so the whole text scrubs as its excerpts do (review r5, P3-2: `--1_token` and `-1_kenCookie:`). The folds' per-line answer is per thread and dropped when the scrub ends. Stated, not fixed: a quoted header value ending in a secret-named flag whose fold onto the next line starts with `=` or `:` shows the next word in the excerpt, whole, `clean` and `detail_line` modes where both references hide it (review r5, P3-4, which found it in `detail_line` only), beside the converse, which is now one of two (round 6 fixes it and removes the exception). Named, as the folded-line trade the clause states: the trailing words daemon.log's line or `detail_line` now keeps after a header this round redacts in the first scrub (3 of the round's 243,680 texts; review r5 counted 27 in its own, and review r6 763 of its 53,825 line-start texts; round 6 restates the scale per corpus), each of which both references show.
- C-23.14 (2026-10-25, D-SCR2 fix round 4, #239 review r4; `docs/reports/2026-10-18-scrub-inline-headers.md`, "Fix round 4"): a fold that starts at a header's name takes nothing where a key, flag or `Bearer` ends that header's line and owns a value past it, so the line's own header rules read it and a header whose whole value is a lone flag takes the value on the line after, whatever fold took its line (it took the line short of the flag, and the header rules, past it, never read the flag's value); the part of a header's name before its header word goes wherever a header rule reads the name, a line-start name parted from its colon by a line break or other whitespace included (only an inline name lost it); a name left starting with `[REDACTED]` at its line's start is read as a line-start name, so daemon.log's folded line keeps nothing of its line. Corrected, as review r4 found them false or incomplete: the lone-flag sentence (for folds; review r5 found it false for a refused `Bearer`'s line and a header whose value starts on the next line, and review r6 for two cases round 6 names), the prefix rule's "at a line start too" (now where it reads a name, and how), and the cost of failing closed, which now names each kind with an example: a digit-named flag's chained fold, the fold of a header's name and line past a quoted name's closing quote, and a name prefix that holds a digit. Found this round beyond review r4, and fixed: no fold, no line a refused `Bearer` takes and no run the `Bearer` rule redacts before a key's credential word reaches into a header the line-start rule reads on a later line, whose name a line break (or, past a fold's run, other whitespace than spaces and tabs) parts from its colon; they ended inside the name or took it whole, so no header rule read it, and the value's second word showed where both references hide it (at c469f44b6, 1,560 of the round's 5,760-cell matrix). And the folds at header names on one line read once whether a key, flag or `Bearer` ends it: with P3-1's fix each read it afresh, walking the line's last identifier, which made a line of such folds before a long flag quadratic (80,010 characters took 12.5 s, against 19 ms at c469f44b6).
- C-23.14 (2026-10-24, D-SCR2 fix round 3, #239 review r3; `docs/reports/2026-10-18-scrub-inline-headers.md`, "Fix round 3"): the value a secret-named flag's fold takes, and a refused `Bearer`'s next line, end before any credential flag that ends them, a secret-named one included, which the flag rule takes with its own folded value (they took such a flag and left its value to no rule); a folded value that starts at a header's name takes that header's whole line (it took the name and left the rest of the line to no rule); the part of a header's name before its header word goes when it reads as a secret; and a flag that whitespace parts from the closing quote ending a quoted inline header's value owns the quoted value after that quote. Corrected, as review r3 found them false: the converse sentence now says it is the one value known to show, over the generators the tests run; the fold sentence says where a fold ends and what it takes at a header's name; the refused-`Bearer` line ends where a fold does. Stated: failing closed's over-redaction, with two examples.
- C-4.2, C-5.5 (2026-10-27, D-QUAR-START1 D4; `docs/reports/2026-10-27-quar-start1-census-under-load.md`): start grace's extension is no longer ended by a census that is unverifiable only because the marker reads of processes other than the guardian failed. Every census reads every process's marker, and on the shared host a newborn of this user refuses that read in its first milliseconds (EIO or EINVAL; each was read whole, or gone, soon after): 20 of 900 whole-host passes met one at load 19 to 43, and at load 94 to 101 the extension ended at such a census in 5 of 6 runs of `tests/fake/test_quar_start1_daemon.py`, KILLing a guardian that was alive and still starting, which D-QUAR-START1 (a) exists to prevent. The census now names those processes (`markers_unread`) and stays unverifiable, so it is still never taken as empty (`containment_empty`). The extension needs only the guardian's own reads: it signals, ends and releases nothing and is bounded; and before `start.json` the guardian has launched nothing, and its identity readers run without its markers (`tests/unit/test_quar_start1_census.py` executes that premise). A failed read of the guardian's own marker, or any other error, still ends the extension. The guardian's own `sysctl` and `ps` children, which sit in its group for a few milliseconds a start (about 14 ms at load 31), were a second way a census met a healthy guardian with another member and ended it; they are admitted beside it as identity readers: an unmarked direct child in its group whose state is neither stopped nor a zombie. A marked child, a grandchild, a child in a group of its own, a stopped one, one with no state, and any member the table did not shape still end it. The event names what was admitted (`markers_unread`, `identity_readers`).
- C-12.3 (2026-10-27, D-ENV3; `docs/reports/2026-10-27-env3-codex-shell-snapshot.md`): a Codex lane command's environment held ANTHROPIC_API_KEY although the daemon had dropped it from the launch (D-ENV1). Codex's shell snapshot, a stable feature on by default in codex-cli 0.159.0, runs the person's `~/.zshrc` and replays everything it exports before each command, after Subfleet's selection and Codex's own `shell_environment_policy`; a failed test's environment dump then sent the key to OpenAI as model input. Every Codex launch that runs a model turn now pins the snapshot, its successor and the login profile off, and no provider launch carries another vendor's credential, even when `env.pass` or the launch's additions name it. Review r1 (GPT-6.1 Sol): a name holding both vendors' words had passed either provider's filter, and the isolated review's metadata inspection had selected from unfiltered additions. Both vendors now drop such a name, `ProviderEnvironment.additions` filters at the source, and the inspection selects as a Codex process. The contract no longer says a command's environment is the launch's: login-shell startup files still run. Review r2 approved, with two P3s: the test's argv checker now reads `-cV`, `-c=V`, `--config V` and `--config=V` as well as `-c V`, and `resume`'s own options, and the report's claims are corrected. Review r3 approved the merge with `release/217`, with one P3: Codex trims whitespace around a key, and the checker now does too.
- C-4.2, C-5.2, C-2.3 (2026-10-26, D-QUAR-START1, folded into D-KILL4 fix round 2b of #250; `docs/reports/2026-10-20-kill4-terminal-containment.md`): (a) at start grace's expiry a guardian that is alive, running (not ps state `T`) and verified by its recorded identity, and is the verifiable census's only member, has its deadline extended at the census's pacing until `START_GRACE_MAX_S` (60 s, 6 × `start_grace_s`) after the deadline began; a stopped guardian, an unverifiable census or any other member never extends, and past the bound the existing branch runs unchanged. (b) The abandon handshake: before the daemon ends or signals an attempt with no `start.json` it publishes `abandon.json` and only then reads `start.json` for the last time; the guardian publishes `start.json` and only then looks for the marker before it spawns (and once its launch gate opens), and on finding it spawns nothing and publishes an abandoned exit receipt (`rc` 127, `spawn_error`, `abandoned`), which ends the attempt unlaunched. A `start.json` found by the daemon's last read means the attempt may have launched, and the full protocol contains it. Incident: 2026-10-26, `20261026-104634-ce1-review-r3/a1`, quarantined at 10 s under load 60 to 100, ended `lost` and rerun, while its guardian launched the provider 21 s later and it ran 13 minutes for output nobody could accept. Measured on this host for the bound: see the report's fix round 2b section.
- C-4.2, C-5.5, C-5.9 (2026-10-26, D-KILL4 fix round 2b, review r2 of #250's P3-1): a start grace whose kill protocol ends with no member left (cwd holders aside) ends unlaunched (`failed`, `starting-no-receipt`, the job queued again while it has attempts left), with no cwd wait, whether or not a process sits in the worktree. It had ended `lost` with no retry for a writable job with nobody there, and `failed` and queued again when an operator's shell was there, which broke C-5.5's "a cwd-only census never changes an attempt's verdict".
- C-5.5 (2026-10-26, D-KILL4 fix round 2b, review r2 of #250's P3-3): a hold is per census. It never authorizes a signal and vetoes none either, so a later census's own attribution (the walk from a counted seed among others) supersedes an earlier hold, and no process is signalled from a census that held it. The code already worked so; the kill4 property had asserted that a process any census held is never signalled, which a random seed falsified on that shape, and now asserts the per-census rule.
- C-5.7 (2026-10-26, D-KILL4 fix round 2b, review r2 of #250's P3-2): `kill` on a finished job whose census is verifiable and has nothing C-5.4 lets the protocol signal runs no protocol and answers `already finished`, naming the members as held, never signalled; `--confirm-dead` no longer points to `kill` for them. `--force-release` resolves only a quarantine.
- C-5.4, C-5.5 (2026-10-26, D-KILL4 fix round 2a, #250 merged with #240 at `8fe3413ef`, which carries #259's D-QUAR-REUSE1; review r2 of #250, check 4; `docs/reports/2026-10-20-kill4-terminal-containment.md`): D-KILL4's group rule and D-QUAR-REUSE1's boot scope are one rule. An attempt whose recorded boot is known to be over has its recorded group excluded by the boot scope before D-KILL4's leader check, so the leader is recorded once, for the attempt's boot, never as `group of another boot`, and the group's members hold nothing; the leader check decides for an attempt on this boot or with an unknown boot. D-KILL4's stored keys take the boot scope that owned, responsible and counted identities have: `chain_identities`, `held_parents`, `held_marked` and taint written into `unverified_identities` receive no identity of another boot than the attempt's, a stored one of another boot is never re-added, held or matched, and an owned identity of another boot attributes no signal target. A held parent is held again only when its boot matches as well as its start. What the kill protocol's censuses carry within one protocol run is this boot's, is not stored, and is held as before.

- C-11.10 (2026-10-27, #260 fix round 5, R5-1): each hold ending reads the job row's clock inside its transaction, applies that ending at most once, and never restores a pass's stale copy over a newer hold. T6 counts the gap between holds once, including a mid-pass commit.
- C-11.10 (2026-10-27, #260 fix round 5, R5-3): named legacy-recheck, replacement-expiry and until-expiry probes pin the existing rules and kill each request-clock or job-clock mutant; all handler judgments use clocks read after writer acquisition.
- C-11.10 (2026-10-27, #260 fix round 5, R5-4a): a quarantined probe's wait ends by verified-empty recovery to `capacity`, then admission takes the hold, or by a person cancelling the job. Confirm-dead answers `not quarantined` and leaves the probe wait `uncertain`; the round-4 entry is corrected.
- C-11.10 (2026-10-27, #260 fix round 5, R5-4b): the legacy audit-stamp fallback is never early while UTC runs forward, may start a held legacy retry at most 60 s late, and is earlier by a backward clock step between the deadline read and audit stamp. The labelled step-back pin deliberately starts at 155 against 160.

- C-11.10 (2026-10-26, #260 fix round 4, R4-1): the door re-reads the current hold inside its move/take transaction, including a release or replacement since the snapshot, and never writes a stale take.
- C-11.10 (2026-10-26, #260 fix round 4, R4-2): legacy retries use their own `attempt.accepted` stamp plus `TRANSIENT_RETRY_DELAY_S`; neither the provider exit nor a rewritten job recheck is their finalization deadline.
- C-11.10 (2026-10-26, #260 fix round 4, R4-3): hold and release sample their effective clocks after acquiring the writer; validation, expiry and replacement use that clock.
- C-11.10 (2026-10-26, #260 fix round 4, R4-4): a quarantined probe's wait ends through a person cancelling the job or an automatic verified-empty census; recovery to `capacity` lets admission take the active hold (corrected in fix round 5, R5-4a).

- C-11.10, C-6.11, C-16.2, C-17.1, C-17.4 (2026-10-24, D-TASKHOLD1): an operator holds one task at admission instead of whole lanes. A compute booking that asks for no new builds (uk-data's batch releases do) could be honoured only by keeping a provider's lanes closed while a build waited on it, which parked every review, lookup and research job on those lanes as well; on 2026-10-24, before builds were made to opt in, one waiting build held all eight Claude ramp lanes for hours (Max: available model capacity should never sit idle behind our own gates). `subfleet tasks hold <task> --until <iso> --note <text>` and `tasks release <task>` (op `tasks`) now keep a new attempt of a detached job of that task from starting, and nothing else: other tasks and conversation turns are placed as with no hold, and running attempts are never touched. A hold is a `leases` row, so the store stays at schema 7. `status` shows a `task holds` section and `status --json` a `task_holds` list; a held job waits `task-held`. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-24-task-hold.md`.
- C-11.10, C-6.11, C-17.4 (2026-10-25, #260 review r1 P2-1, P2-2, P2-3, P3-1, P3-2 and P3-3, D-TASKHOLD1 fix round 1): a task hold leaves a held job's clocks alone and makes no reset demand. Review r1 executed three ways a hold still failed or cost something: a pinned job's 60 s grace kept running while it was held, so a job held at t=10 and released at t=500, or expired at t=1000, failed at once with rc 3; a release or expiry cleared a transient retry's backoff, so a retry due at t=60 started at t=15 or t=20; and a job held after it began waiting on capacity stayed reset-credit demand until a pass looked at it again, so the reset timer spent a gift credit for it. Now the grace counts only time not held and the backoff is kept apart from the hold's clock, each given back at the hold's end (invariant T6), and `_reset_demand` and the reset action's pending check leave held jobs out (T7). Also: offline status counts a hold's waiting jobs over the whole store, not its 50 listed jobs; a note with a control character anywhere is refused before it is trimmed, in the CLI and the daemon alike; `why`'s release hint quotes the task as one argument after `--`; and C-6.11's list of what is not admission's to place names `task-held`. C-11.10 no longer says the store stays at schema 7: release/217, merged in at f6612e160, moved it to schema 9 with job and attempt columns only, and a hold still adds no schema change. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-24-task-hold.md`, "Fix round 1".
- C-11.10 (2026-10-26, #260 review r2 F1, F2, F4 and F5, D-TASKHOLD1 fix round 2): a hold pauses its jobs' clocks from its commit, and keeps only a retry deadline still ahead. Review r2 executed two clocks C-11.10 overstated. A hold that ended before any admission pass looked (the daemon stopped, paced, or still recovering) never paused a grace: a stuck job held at t=10 with no pass failed rc 3 at the hold's end, released at t=500 or expired at t=1000, a restart included, because only a pass took a job. And a hold kept any capacity clock of a job whose last attempt ended `transient` as its retry deadline, a C-6.10 recheck long after the 60 s backoff included, and dropped the capacity wait, so a retry held at t=92 and released at t=93, its slot freed at t=93, started at t=121, where the unheld control started at t=93. Now the hold's own transaction takes every job of the task that waits or is queued (a job still held for an earlier hold that ended unseen is given its clocks back from that end first), as a release gives them back in its own, and a take keeps the job's clock only while its latest unsteered `transient` end plus 60 s is still ahead; otherwise the job is queued at the hold's end. With the commit's take, a held job no longer reaches `_reset_demand` or the reset action's pending transaction on a capacity wait; their hold checks stay behind it, for a job a look under way when the hold committed wrote back to one. C-11.10 also now says what a release after the hold's end does (it deletes the row and records `task.released` with `was_held` false), not that a release with no hold records nothing, and `subfleet/task_holds.py` and the report no longer say the store stays at schema 7. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-24-task-hold.md`, "Fix round 2".
- C-11.10 (2026-10-26, #260 review r3 R3-1, R3-2 and R3-3, D-TASKHOLD1 fix round 3): no admission write overrides a committed hold, and a hold keeps the retry deadline the finalizer wrote. Review r3 executed three gaps. A look already under way when a hold committed wrote its own wait over `task-held`, which round 2 left to the next pass: an episode opened at t=10 over the commit's take, with its capacity wait due at 70, failed the job at 70 though the hold was released at 50 (and at once when the hold expired at 1000 and a pass came at 1030), and a pin check stopped at 59 failed rc 3 at 60 a job whose grace the commit had paused with 1 s left. Now every admission transaction that writes a job's wait, opens its episode or fails it on a clock reads the hold in that transaction and, held, takes the job instead (the episode opens paused), so the window C-11.10 stated is gone: the job fails at 110, 1060 and 501. A finalizer that ran at t=100 for a provider exit at 0 set the retry due at 160, while a take judged the deadline as the exit plus 60, passed, so a hold at 110 released at 111 started the retry at 111; the finalizer now records its deadline in the attempt's evidence (`retry_due_at`), and a take keeps exactly that, while it is ahead (no migration: `evidence_json` is free-form). Round 2's claim that the reset checks could no longer decide alone was disproved; C-11.10 now names the state that reaches them, a held job's retry whose attempt was live at the commit, and C-11.10 says `was_held` is the release's response field, not the event's. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-24-task-hold.md`, "Fix round 3".
- C-11.4 (2026-10-26, D-PROBE-DEADLINE1): admission probes take their durable deadline from `admission.probe_deadline_s` at reservation, 180 s by default (a whole number of seconds from 1 to 900; the loader refuses every float, because `deadline_at` is compared at whole seconds and a 0.5 s deadline stopped its probe at its reservation, review of #269). The 60 s deadline clipped Sol successes and stopped 86 of 174 Sol probes and 28 of 134 Opus probes in the 2026-10-26 measurement. A policy reload applies to the next reservation; C-6.10's 60 s retry clock, timers and enrollment keep their own clocks.

- C-5.5 (2026-10-26, D-KILL3 round 7 / PR #240, review r6): round 6's boot-scope sentence said the final holder read is still made once the attempt's boot is over unless a member's read has already proved the root on another boot. That holds only for a root with a recorded unique id that the census has not proved recycled. A root recorded without a unique id loses its responsibility source before the scan and without an error (`procs.py:1051-1071`): at the recycled check when the later boot's snapshot shows its pid, whatever its start, and otherwise at the early start check. A root whose pid the later boot's snapshot shows is recorded under `recycled`, which disables the source. In neither case are its members or its holder read. The sentence, and round 6's entry below, now say so. The clause just before it no longer reads as if such a root's source were kept. `test_c5_5_boot_scope_the_final_holder_read_by_root_shape` counts the holder reads in trackb's shape for each kind of root. No code changed.

- C-5.5, C-5.9 (2026-10-26, D-KILL3 round 6 / PR #240, review r5): a cancel requested by a thread that already holds the store's write lock takes no cwd scan. The wall's cascade is requested inside the transaction that settles the parent's attempt, at quarantine resolution and at finalization, and its scan of every process's cwd held the store's lock and `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` there (0.65 to 3.70 s on r5's host); each job it ends now keeps its worktree row with no pid line, as for a scan that cannot be read, and the cwd worker settles it. The check is the thread's own hold, not the store's transaction depth, which another thread's transaction also raises. A cancel no longer scans a finished job, one whose export is pending included, which its transaction leaves alone. C-5.5's cost sentence now counts the cwd read's own pid listing, its per-pid reads and its path resolutions, and says when the final holder read is skipped; its boot-scope sentence now says that once the attempt's boot is over, for a root with a recorded unique id, that read is still made unless the census proved the root recycled or a member's read proved the other boot, and only its failure is dropped, while a root recorded without one, whose start is absent or changed, drops the responsibility source without an error, so neither its members nor its holder are read (`procs.py`; #259 r1's P3-1, which said no check was made; corrected in round 7).

- C-5.5, C-5.9, C-12.10, C-13.4 (2026-10-25, D-KILL3 round 4 / PR #240, review r4): every ending converts the worktree lease. A job queued again holding its worktree lease (start grace found only a cwd observer) and then ended from the queue (cancelled while it waits, failed while it prepares its retry, or an unlaunched attempt with no census, cancelled before launch included) scans the worktree before the ending transaction, as the export does, and keeps the folder as the same reader row, also on an unreadable scan, with the pids in its notice; round 3 dropped every lease there, so the folder had no row. An attempt's TMPDIR is released from its census's containment alone: a cwd observer is in the worktree, not the scratch, so it no longer holds the TMPDIR (1.56 GB in #246's measurement) or costs a census each sweep, and the attempt's own processes and an unverifiable census still hold it. Start grace's census is the unlaunched cleanup's too, one census fewer. The cwd clock never restarts sooner than inspect_interval_s after the clock that allowed the census before it: a loss's clock runs from when its inspection fell due, which lags when the attempt is given a shared table that expires early, and at 254 ms a census the loss path took 90 censuses in 30 s against Invariant 5's 61. C-5.9's quarantine sentence now says the worktree stays held as a reader row, and the round-3 entry below says what round 1 did to a turn.

- C-5.5, C-5.9, C-13.4 (2026-10-24, D-KILL3 round 3 / PR #240, review r3): a finished job's worktree kept for cwd observers is a reader row on that folder (`worktree-read:<folder>:<job id>`, held by `cwd:<job id>`), converted from the job's worktree lease in the transaction that releases its other leases, so it keeps retention from removing the folder and excludes no writer. Round 1 kept the exclusive lease while someone had a shell there, which refused `subfleet resume` of the job at submit with `worktree-lease` and held any writable turn into its worktree at admission with `lease-held` (corrected in round 4: turns skip submit's conflict check). The cwd worker releases only those rows, once the job and its attempts hold nothing else, and never a writer's key or another job's row; a job queued again after an unlaunched attempt keeps its own lease. C-6.5's index is unchanged: no refusal was added. The receipt-less loss path is paced too: one census per due inspection, from which the loss is decided, and one `attempt.lost_end`.

- C-5.4, C-5.5, C-5.6, C-5.7 (2026-10-23, D-KILL4 fix round 1, review r1 of #250; `docs/reports/2026-10-20-kill4-terminal-containment.md`): a recorded group's members are signal targets only when the census verified its leader alive with its recorded birth and boot, or by an owned identity; a leaderless group's members only hold the attempt, a group recorded in another boot is no source, and one whose boot cannot be compared only holds the attempt. An expired start grace without start.json KILLs the group at once, and an unlaunched guardian outside it alone; the guardian is never continued; the group's TERM is credited only to the census before it, and a later joiner gets a TERM of its own before any continue. Held parents release once no census sees a marked process name them (the kill protocol's censuses count as one) and kill answers name them. Parent-chain ancestors are kept by birth with their reasons on every path and are never walk seeds. Taint is kept by identity. A stopped process with TERM's default action ends on the TERM alone. C-5.5's sentence on probes holding an ancestor is removed: probes run no parent chain. Markers are signal authority wherever a process runs, an accepted over-reach.

- C-5.5, C-5.9 (2026-10-23, D-KILL3 round 2 / PR #240): the cwd worker releases only allocated worktree holds after export, push and every other lease settle, with an atomic eligibility recheck. Cwd-only finalization and start-grace censuses run at inspect_interval_s; their 30 s wait restarts with the daemon. Quarantine resolution preserves cwd holds while using containment alone for confirmation and salvage, and the wall records cwd-only loss without a kill. C-5.5 names the terminal recheck after a verdict; the P2-2 entry below now reflects persisted counted_absent state.

- C-4.2, C-5.4, C-5.5, C-5.6, C-5.7, C-5.9 (2026-10-22, D-KILL4; incidents 2026-10-20 `hub-build-mi-household-members-std/a1`, `hub-us9981-review-r3b/a1`, `hub-us10299-review-shell-b/a2` and 2026-10-22's 13 stopped escapees; `docs/reports/2026-10-20-kill4-terminal-containment.md`): no attempt ends `failed`, `lost`, `interrupted` or `cancelled` with live census members. An expired start grace with members, writers after the exit receipt and a dead guardian with members run C-5.6's kill protocol before anything follows; verified empty finalizes as before (a start-grace one as a loss, so no retry is reserved before then), survivors quarantine. The census is now the protocol's signal authority (C-5.4): every member with a captured identity, re-checked by C-5.3 just before each signal, except a member only the walk or responsibility found in an unverifiable census, or first recorded by one (`unverified_identities`); never launchd, the daemon, its parent or another attempt's guardian. **C-5.5's signal sentence is amended deliberately:** a cwd inside the worktree now authorizes a signal only for an ancestor of a marked census member (the parent-chain rule), never for a cwd holder alone; cwd-only holders keep #240's rule and never change a verdict. The parent-chain rule adds a marked member's same-uid ancestors whose cwd is in the job's own worktree, and holds (never signals) the first that does not qualify. C-5.6 continues a stopped member right after its TERM and nothing else. C-5.7: plain `kill` re-contains a finished job whose last attempt's census is not empty, and `--confirm-dead` never reports dead while it is. A gate-closed guardian is reaped by its handle before its attempt ends.

- C-5.12 (2026-10-22, D-KILL3 / PR #223 integration): counted expiry and last_observed_alive_at share the distinct-table gate. A repeated shared table changes neither the observation clock nor absence age; a fresh liveness observation remains durable, coalesced with identity changes when possible. Start grace keeps its cwd-only wait after inspected wall handling.

- C-5.9 (2026-10-22, D-KILL3 round 1, terminal recheck follow-up): once a verdict is recorded, cwd scans for its retained worktree run at inspect_interval_s, not on every control-loop tick. Live holders and failed scans keep their lease and the next due time. The offer query starts from active lease keys, and clock entries disappear with their leases.

- C-5.12 (2026-10-22, D-KILL3 round 1 restart follow-up): first-miss tracking is persisted as a bounded counted_absent map beside the recorded birth. This replaces the first in-memory implementation, so restarting between inspections cannot postpone expiry indefinitely. Only deduplication of the current table is in daemon memory; a restart supplies a new table.

- C-5.5, C-5.9 (2026-10-22, D-KILL3 round 1, P2-1): cwd-only evidence holds only the worktree. Finalization and start grace re-take the census for up to 30 s, then use the attempt's own outcome, preserving receipt, rc and deliverable. The worktree lease survives until a fresh cwd scan clears it, including after restart; notices name pids only. Counted identities and cwd holders never authorize a signal.
- C-5.12 (2026-10-22, D-KILL3 round 1, P2-2): counted identities absent or provably recycled in two consecutive distinct inspection tables expire. A live recorded birth never expires, an unchanged table writes nothing, and a deciding census keeps its seed snapshot. JSON evidence is retained with bounded contents; first-miss tracking is a bounded persisted counted_absent map paired with the recorded birth and survives restart; only current-table deduplication is in daemon memory.
- C-5.5, C-13.4 (2026-10-22, D-KILL3 round 1, P3): cwd comparisons use canonical disk spellings as admission does, including case aliases on APFS. Regressions cover observers arriving after a move and a retirement resumed from quarantined.

- C-5.5, C-13.4 (2026-10-19, D-KILL3; `docs/reports/2026-10-19-kill3.md`): a live cwd inside a job-owned worktree is a hold on containment and retention, including an operator's shell or editor. The in-process read compares resolved paths and records only `pid <pid>: cwd in worktree`; it never signals, grants ownership or seeds later walks. In-place workdirs are excluded.

- C-5.5, C-5.12 (2026-10-19, D-KILL3; `docs/reports/2026-10-19-kill3.md`): steady-state inspection keeps walk descendants outside the group as `counted_identities`, so a hidden-marker runner seen before self-exit or a turn stop remains counted at finalization. A runner born and reparented between inspections remains a documented limitation. The D-KILL2 sentence now follows the responsibility birth-record description, restoring the antecedent of "Its live state".
- C-29.7 (2026-10-26, D-STEER-PUMP1 fix round 1, #265 review r1's P2; `docs/reports/2026-10-26-steer-pump.md`): Send now names the turn its tray row was offered for, captured with the action (`QueuedMessage.steerInto`). It named the turn live when the click's callback ran instead: none while the app showed no live turn, so a click that landed after its turn ended, once the next turn had started in the daemon, sent no `into`; with that turn's runner not ready the steer was refused `not-steerable` at once, and with it ready the message joined that turn. The daemon answers a steer whose `into` has ended `no-live-turn` before any runner check, so the message now stays queued, its status line saying why. The clause's last sentence, that a journaled steer names its turn, did not hold for Send now; it now holds for Send now and for Return, which steers only when it can name the turn (the composer already asked to steer only then).
- C-5.5 (2026-10-24, D-QUAR-REUSE1; incident 2026-10-24, `20261014-071003-trackb-d1408-review5/a1`; `docs/reports/2026-10-24-quarantine-boot-seeds.md`): seeds are scoped to the attempt's recorded boot. The attempt stayed quarantined after `kill --confirm-dead`: its exit receipt named `child_pid 83026` with no identity, and on the current boot 83026 was an unrelated `tail -40`, which the walk seeded because an identity-less live pid cannot be proved recycled (`recorded identity unavailable for pid 83026`); `_contain` then kept the stranger's current-boot identity in `counted_identities`, so every later census seeded it as a matching recorded survivor. Now, when the attempt's boot is a boot-session UUID that is not the current boot's, no recorded pid seeds the walk and the recorded group is excluded, an identity-less seed is recorded under `recycled` for `different boot identity (attempt's boot)` without an error, `_contain` keeps no identity from another boot than the attempt's, and a stored identity from another boot is not the attempt's (never a seed, never counted, named under `other_boot`). The attempt's responsibility root is its guardian, so on a later boot neither of the root's identity checks holds it: one recorded without a unique id (the launch's metadata read failed) no longer reports `responsibility root identity unavailable` for its free pid, which held such an attempt for as long as that pid stayed free, and a known one needs no final holder read. An unknown boot keeps the conservative answer, and on the attempt's own boot the census is unchanged (differential test against 15bd8c172). The census may read the boot identity once more, when a seed of an attempt with a recorded UUID shows in the snapshot or a check of its root fails.
- C-13.1 (2026-10-24, D-SALV-MISSING1): a worktree that no longer exists has nothing to salvage. Salvage records `worktree already gone: nothing to salvage` (receipt `gone: true`, evidence and audit event `salvage_gone`, the notice line) instead of a `git rev-parse` failure and a notice that the worktree is kept.

- C-9.6 (2026-10-26, D-RAMP-HOLD-LABEL; `docs/reports/2026-10-26-hold-note.md`): operator holds use one wording everywhere, `<home>: held by the operator[: <note>] (until <T> at the latest)`, so the ramp's six-hour safety bound reads as a bound. Conversation waits gain the `held` kind; pin refusals state the hold separately from any provider limit. Review r1 fixes (build round 3): ordinary and pinned conversation waits retain all applicable closures in canonical order, keeping every hold note and bound separate from provider resets; legacy #263 acknowledgements use the daemon's UTC whole-second normalization; historical pin evidence without `source_event` shows no note, while present invalid sources remain withheld. The hold acknowledgement and `lane.held` event describe the stored row, including an earlier/equal refresh that kept its end and note; an end at or before now is refused before any write. D-STATUS-SRC1's storage, validation, verdict, app label and alert rules remain.
- C-9.6, C-17.1, C-18.4 (2026-10-24, D-STATUS-SRC1; `docs/reports/2026-10-24-status-hold-source.md`): status and the alerts name the source of every lane closure. On 2026-10-24 the Claude lanes started no jobs for nearly 8 hours and the Codex lanes were held from 21:51Z; every hold was a hub controller's `operator-hold` (load, running caps, disk), yet `status`, `status.json` and the notices said the accounts were out: `lane_verdict` called any open account-scope closure `limited`, every closure but `auth-dead` raised "<home> <scope> limited", and the fleet-empty body said every lane was "exhausted, unavailable, or unknown". A hold now reads `held`, raises no per-lane alert, and is counted apart in the fleet-empty body, which is `warn` when every lane is held; `lanes hold --note` records why, in the closure's existing `source_event`; closure lines say what each closure is, and closure rows in `status --json` carry `kind` and `note`. `dispatchable` answers as it did for every lane (a differential property test against release/217's). Review r1 of #263 (2026-10-25): a hold note the C-23.14 scrubber would change is refused with a rule of its own and named in no refusal, log line or row, and every reader withholds one stored outside `lanes hold`; an account closure refuses a lane before its readings are read, in release/217's order, so a lane with malformed evidence answers or raises as it did; the `lanes hold` answer's nested closure rows carry `kind` and `note` too. Review r2 of #263 (2026-10-26): a note is also refused when the scrubber would change it as JSON writes it (a URL password just after a letter outside ASCII); a hold's stored `source_event` is read strictly, so any but `operator`, or `operator: ` and a note the rule accepts as it stands, is withheld in every reader of closure rows and in the route decisions `why`, `run --dry-run` and `runs show`; and C-9.6 says the source guard is syntactic only, and, since review r3 (2026-10-26), states exactly what it catches and names each miss a test case pins.
- C-10.4, C-12.3 (2026-10-25, D-ENV2 P3, review r2 of #248's P3-1 and P3-2): C-10.4 said no transfer leaves a row a store rebuilt from the file would refuse, but a transfer repairs only its own lane's rows, by design, and leaves another lane's refused row as it was; it now says so, and that startup and `--check` report such a row. The code is unchanged. The Codex isolated-review inspection and every local helper the daemon starts (salvage, `retention_git`, gate merge, the conversation catalog, wakes) selected from the daemon's environment with no withheld names, though C-12.3 named them, so a harness hook stored as a lane's credential, or an ignored row's hook, reached them. A helper scope now carries the daemon's withheld names beside its policy, read at each selection, and the Codex adapter passes its own withheld names into the environment the inspection selects from. Outside a scope (the CLI, `daemon start`'s parent) nothing changes. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-24-env2-p3.md`.
- C-6.13 (2026-10-23, #241 review r3 follow-up): retain the resolved driver handle for steady reads, reject and release detached handles, and capture one device ID per baseline scan. The existing unknown-read preservation rule is unchanged; failed root stat or lookup now obeys it. Cost and regression evidence: `docs/reports/2026-10-20-mg2-cpu-idle-guard.md`.
- C-25.7 (2026-10-25, #221 review r8 P3-1 and P3-2, D-SH1): the lint's second rule is stated as it runs: no caught exception's words reach any write past the handler, a standard stream or `os.write` (`_is_raw_write` in `tests/unit/test_daemon_log_sites.py`), not only `os.write`. The two own-process entry points are exempt from the first rule only; inside them a write that passes a caught exception's words is still a finding. The rule for a daemon module's children is its own sentence again, not part of the exemption.
- C-25.7 (2026-10-25, #221 review r7 P3-1, D-SH1): the clause names the lint's two own-process entry points, `publish_rule.hook_main` and `review_publish.wrapper_main`, which r6's P2-1 fix exempted only in `tests/unit/test_daemon_log_sites.py` (`OWN_PROCESS_ENTRY_POINTS`). Their modules are ones a daemon loads through the adapters, so the clause's reason for its other exemptions (none of them is loaded at start) did not cover them. Each runs only in its own process, the generated review hook script or a PATH wrapper, so its streams are never daemon.log, and `test_the_own_process_entry_points_run_only_from_the_generated_scripts` keeps both out of every daemon path.
- C-12.4 (2026-10-22, #221 review r4 P2-1 and P2-2, D-SH1 round 10): gh's command and subcommand must be spelled literally on every path. Wherever the command word is gh, a word known only at run time before gh's verb is settled (as its command, its subcommand, or a flag or a flag's value before them), or arguments from `xargs` or GNU `parallel`, make the call no read, refused with the `publish` wording; the fallback had judged gh only when its first word was a known command, so `arch -arm64 gh $v comment`, `arch -arm64 gh ${=a}` and `… | xargs arch -arm64 gh` ran gh past the hook. A run-time command word that may be gh is judged the same way; a text tool's arguments get neither addition. `env`'s options are read as its getopt reads them, so `-S` counts inside a cluster (`env -vS`, `-iS`), and an `-S` value with a backslash or `${` is refused (env's own escapes: `env -S 'gh\_pr\_comment'` ran gh). `csh` and `tcsh` are shells to the rule, every listed shell's `-c` counts anywhere in a cluster with both readings of `o` and `O`, and the fallback reads a shell, `env` or `xargs` among its words as a command of its own. `gh -R "$REPO" pr view`, `xargs gh pr view` and `parallel gh pr view ::: 1` are refused as a result; `gh pr view 7 -R "$REPO"` passes.
- C-12.4 (2026-10-22, D-SH1 round 9, found beside #221 review r3c): the publish rule compares command names, and the words it reads as naming gh, in any case. macOS's default volume is case-insensitive, so `GH pr comment 7`, `arch -arm64 GH pr new` and `BASH <<< 'gh pr comment 7'` ran gh and git past a rule that compared names case-sensitively.
- C-12.4 (2026-10-22, #221 review r3c P2 and P3, D-SH1 round 9): the publish rule's fallback and run-time words judge gh as its direct path does. Once a call reaches one of gh's commands as gh's own parser finds it (its built-in aliases, the hidden `auth git-credential`, and any flag before the command or its verb included), it is refused unless it is a read; they had refused only the verbs they could name, so `pr new`, `cs delete`, `gh pr -R o/r comment` and `gh auth git-credential get` passed through `arch` and after `$(command -v gh)`. `-R`/`--repo`/`--hostname` before a verb no longer stop a read (`gh pr -R o/r view 7`), `ls` reads wherever `list` does, and `gh auth git-credential` is a credential call on every path. A run-time word followed by a gh command word is judged as gh whatever program it names, which the clause states.
- C-12.4 (2026-10-20, #221 review r3 P3): the publish hook self-test's per-file digest cache keys on ctime as well as path, device, inode, size and mtime, so an in-place rewrite of the same size with its mtime restored is hashed again and its self-test runs again.
- C-14.2 (2026-10-19, D-SH1 round 8, CI at `f62fdec4a`): a review's publish hook that Codex's hooks/list does not trust is a `publish` verdict with its own reason, `guard-preflight-publish`. It had been a `trust` verdict, whose text called a read-only review "the writable job" and asked to verify TRUST and rerun `subfleet doctor`, though the guard had verified and doctor never checks the publish hook; the repair now points at the attempt's `guard-preflight.json` and a Claude model.
- C-12.3 and C-12.4 (2026-10-19, D-SH1 round 8, #221 merged with #222): the git calls of a review's uncommitted copy select C-12.3's names and add only their scratch index; they had given git the daemon's whole environment. C-12.4 states that a review launch's pinned git denial and marker are launch additions, which neither the allowlist nor its credential-word backstop withholds and the guardian passes on, and `launch_env.LAUNCH_NAMES` lists `SUBFLEET_PUBLISH_DENIED`.
- C-6.7 (2026-10-19, #221 review r2 P3-9, D-SH1 round 7): a writable job's workspace note says that an ignored file the task names is not in its worktree and may be read in the caller's checkout at the same relative path.
- C-12.4 (2026-10-19, #221 review r2 P3-7, D-SH1 round 7): before a publish-denied launch starts, each publish hook its argv installs is fed a `gh pr comment` payload, and the launch is refused (`publish-hook-self-test`) unless the hook denies; passes are cached per daemon run by hook command and file hashes.
- C-12.4 (2026-10-19, #221 review r2 P3-3, P3-4 and P3-6, D-SH1 round 7): every Codex review launch pins `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL`, `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS` and `SUBFLEET_PUBLISH_DENIED` with `-c shell_environment_policy.set`, so a lane's `inherit="core"` cannot drop them; a writable Claude review launch passes `--setting-sources user`, so the reviewed tree's project settings never load. A review cannot install a git-sourced dependency, a fetch loss accepted knowingly.
- C-6.6 (2026-10-19, #221 review r2 P3-1 and P3-2, D-SH1 round 7): a policy review's copy is marked applied once it is all in the worktree; a worktree whose copy has no marker, left by a daemon stopped between the cut and the end of the copy, is cut again before any attempt instead of being reviewed as complete. The fallback's wording says a nested repository is the only untracked path that reaches it: git never lists a FIFO or a socket.
- C-12.4 (2026-10-19, #221 review r2 P2 and P3-5, D-SH1 round 7): the publish rule covers ordinary shell. Here-strings and process substitutions read as a script, `eval` or `sh -c` of a parameter when the text names gh, gh's global flags before a run-time word, leading redirections, glob and brace command words, zsh's `-` and `repeat`, and `env -S` are judged; a fallback judges any word naming gh or git (and assignment, `-c`, `-e`, `--exec` and git config values) by what it names; copying gh under another name is refused, and so is printing the GitHub credential, with its own reason and deny text. `gh api` is judged as gh's flag parser reads it, so an explicit GET with fields, `-H…` and `-q…` clusters, `gh --version` and `gh auth status` are reads. C-12.4 states exactly what remains outside the rule.
- C-6.6, C-6.7 and C-15.1 (2026-10-18, #221 review r1b P2 and P3-8, D-SH1 round 6): a policy `worktree-write` review's worktree now holds the caller's uncommitted and untracked files as of submit, bounded at 1,000 paths and 16 MiB, or the review reads the checkout in place; notices and `runs show` say which. A writable review gets a review preamble, not the build one.
- C-12.3, C-12.4 and C-14.2 (2026-10-18, #221 review r1b P1, P2 and P3-7, D-SH1 round 6): zsh startup in both providers' command paths put Homebrew ahead of the PATH wrappers, so `gh` writes reached the real gh. A job-scoped PreToolUse command rule now refuses them in both providers (Claude `--settings`, Codex a second trusted hook verified by the guard preflight); `gh api` flag clusters with `f`, `F` or `X` are refused except `-XGET`; the read list gains the run, issue, pr list and other reads a review needs; refusing remote fetch is accepted.
- C-20.6 (2026-10-24, D-ST3-P3, PR #242 review r5's P3s; `docs/reports/2026-10-19-st3-isolation.md`): the clause's body now states the rule only its change log had: a HOME spelled with a leading `~` or `~<name>` under a working directory that cannot be read is refused, since its literal reading cannot be judged. `protected_provider_homes` had said "HOME names one of the account's live stores" for it; it now names the unreadable working directory, unless the pwd reading lies in a live root. A test with a deleted working directory pins both guards, and three mutants pin the branch (review r5's M2 among them).
- C-10.4, C-10.5, C-12.3, C-17.8, C-25.7 (2026-10-23, D-ENV2 fix round 1, review r1 of #248's P3s): each came from a refused `lanes.json` row that D-ENV2 lets the daemon start with, where the release before it exited 2. The daemon withheld only the stored lanes' names, so a harness hook that only an ignored row named reached every launch; the daemon now remembers the ignored rows' hooks at startup and both of its withheld sets add them, exactly as for a held lane's hook. `daemon start --check` printed `lanes.json`'s lane ids unscrubbed, in text and `--json`; it prints each row's lane id and variable as `daemon start` prints them, scrubbed, and doctor's held-lane row scrubs each lane's words too. `lanes transfer` set only `owner` on the lane's existing row, so a refused row stayed and a store rebuilt from the file refused startup; a transfer now repairs each of the lane's refused rows to the store's own credential. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-19-env2-followups.md`, "Fix round 1".
- C-1.3, C-10.4, C-10.5, C-11.1, C-12.3, C-17.1, C-17.8 (2026-10-20, D-ENV2, review r5 of #222's P3s): a refused `lanes.json` row stopped the daemon even for a lane already stored, whose row seeding skips, so a store round 4 left came back to exit 2 under launchd's KeepAlive until someone edited the file. Such a row is now ignored with one warning per lane, and the stored-lane rule decides (held, or repaired by a fixed row); only a refused row for a lane the store lacks refuses startup. `lanes transfer` refuses a held lane, whose row it had written into `lanes.json` with the refused variable. Doctor's lane-variable row warns for each held lane, with the `lanes.json` edit that repairs it. Every session probe name is a refused lane variable, so `PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST`, whose value selection reads, is no lane's credential, and resolution refuses one before reading it. C-1.3 says a variable no launch inherits, not one off the allowlist. `daemon start --check` lists each env-kind `lanes.json` row whose variable some launch inherits or the probe reads, reading that file alone. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-19-env2-followups.md`.
- C-20.6 (2026-10-23, D-PRR1 fix round 2, #244 review r2's P3-2): the limit now names the kernel's copy of the start-up environment. Removing a variable unsets it in the process, but `KERN_PROCARGS2` still returns the environment the process started with, so `ps -E` and `peers.process_chain` on the test process still read each inherited value; a child started after the removal holds none. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-21-prune-restore-recipe.md`.
- C-20.6 (2026-10-23, D-PRR1 fix round 2, #244 review r2's P3-1): the clause's limit named only a value copied into a local by hand, but review r2 printed inherited dummies through reads out of the store too: `dict(store)`, `store[name]`, `store._values`, a pickle under `-vv`, and `__reduce_ex__`. The limit now says a test that reads a value out of the store (by name, `dict()`, `_values` or `__getstate__`) or copies one by hand. `RemovedCredentials` now raises `TypeError` when pickled or copied, so `pickle` and `copy` carry no value. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-21-prune-restore-recipe.md`.
- C-20.6 (2026-10-22, D-PRR1 fix round 1, #244 review r1): the store that keeps a test session's removed credentials, `home_paths.RemovedCredentials`, shows their names only in its `repr` and `str`, and is not a `Mapping`, which pytest 9 diffs item by item, values included. The session's baseline snapshot holds no credential-shaped value, and the session check binds the removed names only: it had bound the values, which `-l` printed when it failed. The clause now states what is guaranteed and its limit, in place of "no test, child or report can print one": a test that copies a value into a local by hand, or prints a variable set after configure, can still print it. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-21-prune-restore-recipe.md`.

- C-11.5, C-20.6 (2026-10-22, D-PRR1): the prune pass's restore recipe fences its shell block with a backtick run longer than any inside the block, and never shorter than three, so a backup path that holds backticks cannot end the block early for a Markdown reader. A test session removes every credential-shaped variable before collection and puts each back at cleanup, so none is in the environment a test or an inheriting child sees: pytest's long traceback printed `subprocess.run`'s `kwargs`, a copied environment with them, in a failing test's report. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-21-prune-restore-recipe.md`.

- C-23.14, C-25.7 (2026-10-23, D-SCR2 fix round 2, #239 review r2; `docs/reports/2026-10-18-scrub-inline-headers.md`, "Fix round 2"): no scrub shows a value 473fe2941 or release/217 at c2e080e44 hides, but the stated converse. A credential flag whose name reads as a secret owns nothing and is redacted, by the rule around it or the flag rule, with the value daemon.log's folded line gives it, in every mode (fail closed, the hub's ruling); so does a header whose whole value is a flag, which the merge with D-LOG1 needed: kept, the folded line read that header's value to the record's end, past `cut_whole`'s cut. A header that a refused `Bearer` ends takes the next line; the run glued before a refused `Bearer` flag is redacted, so the key form is the converse's only form; a `Bearer` ending a key's plain value in a quoted match keeps its token; an inline header whose value would start on the next line leaves that line's header to it; the unclosed values on a line read its end once. Excerpts are not cut between a credential flag and a value on a later line. Corrected: the line-start header sentence, the idempotence claim (monotone, not idempotent), the suffix-search sentence and the converse's forms. C-25.7: the leading-header rule reads C-23.14's header names (an identifier ending in `authorization` or `cookie`) by a C scan of the name and its word in at most 13 characters.
- C-23.14 (2026-10-22, D-SCR2 fix round 1, #239 review r1; `docs/reports/2026-10-18-scrub-inline-headers.md`, "Fix round 1"): every input that review found showing a value 473fe2941 or release/217 hides is closed, and the refused-`Bearer` converse, which shows one release/217 hid, is stated. An item that keeps its own value also ends a credential flag's value (a flag glued to its end) and an unclosed quoted value, and one ending a line is read at the line's last visible character. The inline rule's second reading always runs and reads names at a line start, so a header's name that another header's value would take gets a header's value, and an excerpt scrubs as the same rules scrub the whole block. Corrected: the swallowed-header sentence, the excerpt sentence (the within-line rules over the whole block, not a whole-text scrub), and the flag sentence (a later line past blank lines; a shell continuation shows in every mode).
- C-23.14 (2026-10-20, D-SCR2, #231 review r1 and #233 review r3; `docs/reports/2026-10-18-scrub-inline-headers.md`): inline credential headers are scrubbed. A name ending in `authorization` or `cookie` anywhere on a line, then `:`, takes a header's value, to the closing quote matching one before the name or to the line's end, so an unquoted one in prose loses the rest of its line; `Proxy-Authorization` is a header at a line start too. A secret that ends in a credential word stays hidden: a header leaves only the word to the named-value rules, and a refused `Bearer` redacts the token before the word, as approvals now mask it. `Bearer` decides once per match, at release/217's cost. A credential key, flag or `Bearer` that another rule's redaction would take keeps its own value, exactly as without that rule; a flag's value on the next line is still read only in daemon.log's folded line. The inline-header limitation D-SCR1 documented is gone.
- C-12.10 (2026-10-25, D-TMP1-P3 r2 and r3, PR #258's CI on macos-15 and its review r1b2): removal now checks the entry under the allocation's name against the record, by the same identity check as after the rename, and sets it to 0700 before renaming it, because macOS 15 refuses to rename a directory without owner write, even within one parent; #258 had changed the mode after the rename, so a locked TMPDIR stayed for ever there. Every mode change removal makes sets exactly 0700 (r3). r2 set the directory's mode plus owner bits by name, so an entry swapped in between the check and the mode change, such as a hard link to a file of this user's outside the temp root, took the folder's group and other bits (review r1b2 P2-1). The change now goes through the directory's descriptor, opened without following a symlink and checked again, and only a directory whose open is refused with EACCES (no owner read) is changed by name. The clause states the bound that holds: an entry swapped in between the check and the mode change can be set to 0700, only if it is this user's (a symlink's own mode, never its target's); it never gains group or other bits. It also states that a crash-left tombstone, which no rename precedes, still gets its mode changed after its check, that nothing inside the tree is renamed, that after a refused removal the folder put back is at 0700, that a put-back the system refuses leaves the entry at its tombstone, logged, that a refused tree is logged with a code of a closed set, and that the journal folder's own entry is fsynced when the folder is first made (review r1b2 check 12). Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-22-attempt-tmpdir.md`, D-TMP1-P3 section, r2 and r3.
- C-12.10 (2026-10-24, D-TMP1-P3, PR #246 review r2 P3-1, P3-2, P3-3, P3-5 and review r3's wording P3s): removal renames to a tombstone name the store recorded first (in the allocation record, or an unknown folder's sighting), and a crash that leaves the folder there is finished by the next release or sweep; a record is released `absent` only when neither its path nor its tombstone holds it. The tree's own top folder is made owner-accessible before it is opened, removal refuses a tree `retention_fs.MAX_DEPTH` levels deep as `size()` does, and an allocation that fails once its folder exists removes the folder. Each allocation's name is in the state root's journal (`tmpdir-intents/`) from before its mkdir until its marker is published, so a folder a crash left unmarked is still this store's and is swept. Retiring a job whose TMPDIR is cleared but not removed logs the folder. The Codex-lane sentence now limits itself to a brief's scratch, and the Claude-lane one says the shared-root path is the brief's to clean up. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-22-attempt-tmpdir.md`, D-TMP1-P3 section.
- C-20.6 (2026-10-23, D-ST3, PR #242 review r4's P2; `docs/reports/2026-10-19-st3-isolation.md`): a HOME spelled with a leading `~` or `~<name>` is judged in both its readings, the home pwd names and the literal path joined to the working directory, which `production_state_root` and `protected_provider_homes` build on (`Path(HOME) / ...`). From a folder inside the account's home, `~/..`-style spellings name that home or a live root only literally; pwd alone missed them, and on Python 3.14 release/217 already did. A `~<name>` naming no user keeps its literal reading; an unreadable working directory counts as a live root. Python 3.12 and 3.13's `Path.home()` refusal of such a HOME no longer stands in for the judgment (`home_paths.spelled_home`).

- C-20.6, C-25.7 (2026-10-23, D-ST3, PR #242 fix round 2, after release/217's #233; `docs/reports/2026-10-19-st3-isolation.md`): `daemon.run_program` writes a test process's refusal through `daemon_log.write_scrubbed`, as `daemon.main` writes its own refusals. It had printed the line to stderr, which is daemon.log under `daemon start`, so the merge failed C-25.7's lint on exactly the line #233 replaced in `main`. The words and exit 7 are unchanged; a refusal names a variable, never its value.
