- C-20.6 (2026-10-23, D-ST3, PR #242 review r2's P3-1): the `subfleetd` console script runs `daemon.run_program`, the entry `python -m subfleet.daemon` runs, so a test process's refusal is exit 7 and one line from either. The script had run `daemon.main`, which raises, so a marked child exited 1 with a traceback; `cli._daemond_argv` launches that script whenever it finds one. The clause names the console script, and its modules `pyproject.toml` (and `subfleet/doctor.py`, which round 1's PYTHONPATH check made one of them).

- C-20.6 (2026-10-23, D-ST3, PR #242 review r2's P3-2): in a test process any override under `/.vol/`, macOS's volfs, which names a file by its device and inode, is refused by any spelling, before any call on it, and a HOME under `/.vol/` counts as one in a live root. Review r2 named a stand-in account's `.subfleet` as `/.vol/<device>/<inode>`, and `client.state_root()` returned it: the check recognizes a live root by its name. The clause now says "by any spelling of its name" and "no call on a live root ... by its name", and states the limit: it judges names, not inodes, so a hard link to a live file is out of scope; a test pins that limit.

- C-20.6 (2026-10-23, D-ST3, PR #242 review r2's P3-3): in a test process an empty override is judged as `.`, the working directory it names, through `home_path` and `checked_override` alike, so a working directory inside a live root is refused, as `.` already was; elsewhere `""` is still returned. The clause had said `""` names the working directory, "never a live root", but nothing judged it. Outside a test process nothing changes.

- C-20.6, C-2.1 (2026-10-22, D-ST3, PR #242 review r1 continued): a relative `SUBFLEET_CODEX_GUARD_CACHE` override is judged against the state root, as `cache_dir` consumes it, through `checked_override(..., relative_to=root)`; all other callers keep their working-directory base. The raw leading-tilde refusal still runs first. This prevents a checkout under `~/.subfleet/worktrees` from making a temporary state root's cache look personal, and refuses a relative cache that actually lies in a live root before resolving it.

- C-20.6 (2026-10-22, D-ST3, PR #242 review r1 continued): the path-variable sweep also covers each entry that `doctor.check_pythonpath` probes from `PYTHONPATH`. A test process applies `checked_override` to every entry before any package-path probe; an interpreter running with `-E` or `-I` still reports the variable unread. Plain production processes retain release's values and exceptions, checked in the fresh-child differential.

- C-20.6 (2026-10-22, D-ST3, PR #242 review r1; `docs/reports/2026-10-19-st3-isolation.md`): every override takes the `~` refusal, and an explicit live root is refused too. `adapters/claude_mcp.global_config_path` read `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` raw, so `~` skipped the refusal and returned `~/.claude.json`, which `os.path.expanduser` makes the live file; it, and the seven other raw reads of a path-valued variable the sweep found (`SUBFLEET_GUARD_TRUST`, `SUBFLEET_CODEX_GUARD_CACHE`, `CODEX_HOME`, `CLAUDE_LANE_AGENT_SECRET`, `SUBFLEET_DAEMON_BIN`, `SUBFLEET_DEV_APP_EXECUTABLE`, `TMPDIR`), go through `home_paths.checked_override`. In a test process an override naming the account's home, a live root or a path inside one is refused by any spelling (after `~` expansion, lexically normalized, and followed through links, without a stat on or beneath a live root); this process's own code is its own. A HOME spelled `~<name>`, `~` or `~/...` is judged through pwd, and a HOME inside a live root keeps the process rule under the seam and is refused by `production_state_root` and `protected_provider_homes`. `compat.v1_binary` refuses its PATH fallback in a test process. The clause now names the `accept_empty` callers, for which `""` names the working directory, as release has it. A refusal is `PersonalPathRefused`, a RuntimeError, and `python -m subfleet.daemon` reports one in one line, exit 7. Production is unchanged.
- C-20.6 (2026-10-20, D-ST3; #222 fix round 5; `docs/reports/2026-10-19-st3-isolation.md`): a pytest process refuses the account's live roots whatever its os.environ holds. D-ST1's refusals read the session from os.environ alone, so four of #222's round-5 unit tests that replaced it with a plain dict naming neither marker read as production, and `cmd_daemon_start` fell back to the person's `~/.subfleet` (chmod, `policy.json`, `lanes.json` and the store read, `daemon.log` opened for append; nothing written). For this process `home_paths.under_pytest()` is now the environment rule or the process rule (`home_paths.pytest_loaded`: `_pytest` or `pytest` in `sys.modules`); a child given an environment keeps the environment rule alone. A test of production behaviour waives the process rule only through the `production_process` fixture, and only while HOME is not the account's own. `home_path` also refuses an override beginning `~` that expands to the account's home; `is_account_home` also matches the lexically normalized spelling and the one `Path.resolve` builds on, so `home/missing/..` counts; and `compat.v1_binary`'s default v1 install (`SUBFLEET_V1_BIN`, now a session path) and `python -m subfleet.prune_decisions`'s default state root go through `home_path`.
- C-25.7 (2026-10-23, D-LOG1, #233 review r6): a margin file that ends in a reminder keeps its last lines. What is left is used where the margin-free readings agree up to the trailing line breaks such a reminder leaves, which the block form does not read, unless those breaks bring a data URI body at the end to the 32 characters its rule needs (the build found that agreeing up to trailing breaks alone let the tail show a 31-character body the whole-text scrub hides). The cost sentences give this tree's measurements in place of review r5's of round 6's code: the four-byte margin shape, whose cost is linear (counted by the count test, timed at 1 to 32 MiB), the other shapes and Python's formatting, and the measured cost of round 7's stronger check. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-17-daemon-log-scrub.md`, follow-ups after merge.
- C-12.10 (2026-10-24, D-TMP1, PR #246 review r2 P3-4): the operational note now says what `getconf DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR` gives on a Codex lane: inside the sandbox it names the attempt's TMPDIR, so either spelling of a brief's scratch is private and lasts one attempt; on a Claude lane the `getconf` path stays in the shared root. It had said such a path was outside a Codex lane's writable roots.
- C-12.10 (2026-10-23, D-TMP1, PR #246 review r1): salvage state never holds an attempt's TMPDIR and nothing reads `tmpdir_keep`; it is held only while its holder is live or quarantined or its census is not verified empty. An absent folder is always released (`outcome: absent`), a verified-empty census records `cleared_at`, which pins nothing, and a held holder gets no repeated census or record. Removal renames to a tombstone and checks identity before deleting, and clears read-only directories inside the tree. Known or unknown, and an unknown folder's age, come from the store (`tmpdir.unknown_seen`), never from the marker or the folder's times. One allocation per attempt across launch tries; an allocation error is a launch failure. TMPDIR has a trailing slash. The startup sweep runs once the daemon is serving. The clause now says that Codex can no longer write the rest of the user temp root, so briefs keep scratch under `$TMPDIR`, that a turn's scratch lasts one turn, and what the placement reserve does and does not cover. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-22-attempt-tmpdir.md`, round-1 fix section.
- C-12.10 (2026-10-22, D-TMP1): every provider attempt, including retries, resumes, turns, enrollment and capacity probes, receives private user-temp scratch. Census-gated cleanup, startup and retention sweeps, salvage holds and durable cleanup receipts keep other attempts safe and account for leftovers. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-22-attempt-tmpdir.md`.
- C-29.7 (2026-10-22, queue tray fix-8, review r4 P2-2): opening hydrates only receipt identities absent from the app and the opening snapshot, in batches of at most 200; reopening fetches no receipt it already holds. Receipts fold with at most one sort per batch, and an unchanged receipt sequence needs no sort. With three queued and 5,000 settled messages, open and reopen each apply in under 50 ms on the main thread.

- C-27.5, C-29.12 (2026-10-22, queue tray fix-8, review r4 P2-1, P3-1): only a reveal that scrolls the timeline suspends its opening pin and catch-up. The end marker alone sets whether the timeline follows; a tray-only reveal leaves it alone. Each search request fires its 250/550 ms reveal pair once its target is present; unrelated row arrivals neither postpone nor repeat it. The duplicate CI flakes (2026-10-17, D-2 and D-RL1) change-log entry is consolidated into one.

- C-29.12 (2026-10-20, queue tray fix-7, CI macOS 15): a reveal targeting a tray row makes every tray row visible as a function of state, independent of background tasks. The tray remains expanded after the reveal is consumed, and scrolling waits for layout to report the target row.

- C-29.7, C-29.12 (2026-10-20, queue tray fix-6, review r3 P3): activating a match in a collapsed queue expands the tray before scrolling its own container to the matched row, including the sixth of six held messages.

- C-27.5, C-29.7 (2026-10-20, queue tray fix-6, review r3 P2, D3): the app’s explicit approval/search row reveals suspend catch-up jumps as well as the opening pin. Later history and event pages preserve that reading position until the person returns to the end.

- C-25.1, C-29.7 (2026-10-20, queue tray fix-6, review r3 P2s): focused refresh uses `conversation.status` only with `conversation.status.v1`; older daemons use the full `conversation.open` re-read. Opening with status support reads every message identity and hydrates missing receipts in batches of at most 200 before publishing the tray count, including queues longer than the opening snapshot.

- C-29.7, C-27.5, C-29.12 (2026-10-16, queue tray fix-4, D1–D3): membership only moves forward, focused pages hydrate every message including daemon repairs through a transcript-free status read, and explicit row reveals suspend the opening pin.
- C-6.13 (2026-10-22, #241 round 2): stat the state root on each disk sample and re-resolve its driver on a root-device change or failed selected-device read. A changed identity discards counters and EWMA; two successful samples on the new device restore service readings without sampler recreation. Baseline availability and age share positive, finite, current-device, 24-hour eligibility. Steady reads select one registry entry without enumerating neighbors.

- C-6.13 (2026-10-22, #241 round 1 calibration follow-up): a restored baseline's age is its calibration observation's age; the minute recording gate persists recording time separately from the snapshot's observation time, so a stale snapshot cannot authorize an early write after restart.

- C-6.13, C-6.11 (2026-10-22, D-MG2 #241 round 1): disk busy now means state-device service milliseconds per operation, with uncapped in-flight requests alongside it; both new signals use bias-corrected 60 s EWMAs on a one-second cadence, startup priming and ten-second recovery. Lost reads preserve baselines and latches while holding nothing. Native reads are absent for null/legacy-only policies. `daemon.status` / `status --json` expose values, units, ages and read cost; bounded minute samples persist quiet-baseline calibration. The proposal has no disk threshold until calibration; live/default policy is unchanged. Restore the 9/27 evidence and correct the old 10/11 disk claim.

- C-6.13 and C-6.11 (2026-10-20, D-MG2, owed for 2.1.11.5): opt-in CPU idle and bounded IOKit disk service-time conditions replace load in the proposed machine guard, with a 60 s CPU EWMA and two-pass release margins. Existing load/memory policy behavior and the shipped null default remain unchanged. Unreadable signals hold nothing, including after a latch; hold records and `why` name the readings and release progress. Sources, costs, calibration and tests: `docs/reports/2026-10-20-mg2-cpu-idle-guard.md`.
- C-25.7 (2026-10-22, D-LOG1, #233 round-7 continuation): replace the salvaged WIP's unfilled measurement tokens with explicitly attributed review-r5 observations, including four-byte margin text and Python's formatting ratios. Timings and fallback frequencies are not acceptance bounds; fresh timing and census completion is recorded separately in the round-7 report. The exact three conditions that keep only the cut's marker, and both sides' earlier change-log entries, remain.
- C-25.7 (2026-10-22, D-LOG1, #233 review r5): what a person reads of a tagged daemon.log takes its reminders out before the cut only where the whole-text scrub's first quoted pass over what is left replaces exactly what it replaces in the file outside them, by the same words, and the same read without a group's margins (round 6 took them out of the unmasked text, so a reminder between a key's separator and a quote opened a value there and the tail showed what the whole-text scrub hides); the clause now says exactly when a file keeps only the cut's marker, where it had also said that none does. The cut's quote regex starts a match only where a run of backslashes starts, so a run no quote follows is read once, not again from each of its backslashes; its regexes are counted by what they read, not by their span; and its cost is stated as the bound the code gives, with the ratios to plain text, the four-byte margin shape among them, and Python's formatting given as measurements. `daemon logs -f -n N` first prints what `daemon logs -n N` prints of the file; its holds apply only to what is written after, and a value still open as following starts shows as `daemon logs -n N` shows it then. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-17-daemon-log-scrub.md`, round 7.
- C-25.7 (2026-10-20, D-LOG1, #233 review r4): the cut's cost is stated as the code gives it: past its windows, a fixed number of scans of the text plus at most 20 needle looks, one for each quote kind open at a candidate cut, each reading the text at most once (round 5 said at most 12 passes in all, which ten kinds open a side read past), counted with a data URI body's match by what it reads, and its worst ratio to plain text measured, 6.7 times on a run of margin-only lines; the cut as of cce8f1af7, Python's formatting and `daemon logs`' cost are restated as measured, one measure each, `daemon logs` on a realistic daemon.log. No reminder tag reaches what the handler writes or what a person reads of daemon.log: the handler writes `[reminder-tag]` for each tag its scrub leaves, and the tail and `daemon logs` read a file they must cut with its reminders resolved first, as the whole-text scrub resolves them, so one tag no longer leaves them only the cut's marker; `cut_whole` keeps its rule for a record, and the clause says why. `daemon logs --follow` holds a private key's first line until it can no longer become one. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-17-daemon-log-scrub.md`, round 6.
- C-25.7 (2026-10-20, D-LOG1, #233 review r3): the record's cut runs its regexes only within its two windows, and past them reads only in C scans, at most 12 passes over the text: a group's margins are blanked within the windows and recognized past them where a look reaches one (C scans over each run of margin-only lines), a reminder tag is found by C scans, a leading header is matched at the first visible character; its output is unchanged (a differential test holds it to the cut as of cce8f1af7), and "tens of milliseconds however long it is" is replaced by the measured ratio to plain text (at most 1.7 times plain text on review r3's shapes, and up to 6.3 times, linear, on a run of margin-only lines, an accepted cost). `daemon start`'s tail keeps its lines where folding lets the scrubber find more, each run written only where the folded rescrub shows it; `daemon logs` prints daemon.log scrubbed the same way, with no raw form, and with `--follow` holds each new line until no match can cross the break after it, its residual limits stated. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-17-daemon-log-scrub.md`, round 5.
- C-25.7 and C-11.1 (2026-10-19, D-LOG1, #233 round 4, after release/217's #222): the merge brought #222's `dbfd33c64`, whose `daemon.main` printed a refused policy to stderr, daemon.log, past the handler, and `daemon start` printed its own pre-spawn refusal raw, so a refused admission key's value reached both. What the daemon writes to stderr itself (a refused policy or roster, a held lock, a retired alias's note) now goes through the handler's formatter (`daemon_log.write_scrubbed`); `daemon start` prints its refusal, its held-lane notes and its tail of daemon.log scrubbed the same way (`daemon_log.scrubbed`), the tail as one record, cut first; no daemon module writes its stdout or stderr but through `daemon_log`, or passes an exception's words to `os.write`, and none starts a child on them, each held by a lint that names the front ends it leaves out. The list of daemon.log's other writers no longer names the held lock's line or the alias note, which are scrubbed now.
- C-25.7 and C-24.10 (2026-10-19, D-LOG1, #233 review r2): the record's cut never falls between a credential flag and the value folding joins to it, reads a private key's split first and last lines as folding does and a group's margins as spaces, keeps only the marker on a side whose window holds more than eight distinct escaped quote kinds, and does work that does not grow with the text; a record's part is read as an exception group's before its cut, or when a line of it starts with a group's margin; a one-line message cut to the marker keeps its level and logger name; a module that builds a message another function logs from a caught exception uses `refusals.scrubbed_words`, its class or its errno; C-5.8a's managed stop snapshot is named among daemon.log's other writers; a refused wake run target is named by its position, never its id. The carve-out for a workspace completion SQLite could not record is removed: since #233 review r1 (`98f2ef0c1`) its notice names the class only (`refusals.fixed_error`).
- C-25.7 and C-26.6 (2026-10-18, D-LOG1, #233 review r1): a steer sent back to the queue because its input or driver failed names the exception's class and errno, never its words, in its `state_reason` and `steer.missed` events; a `wake-refused` event shows the wake module's fixed words or the class; the daemon's handler cuts a record's text to 16,384 characters before it scrubs it, at line breaks no whole-text match crosses, and formats a record before it takes its lock, which only the write holds; an exception group's block hides at least what the same text without Python's margins hides; on Python 3.14 faulthandler's all-threads dump also names threads, each fixed words or ids.
- C-25.7 and C-26.10 (2026-10-18, D-LOG1, #225 reviews r1 and r2): every line and traceback the daemon logs is scrubbed at its handler (`subfleet/daemon_log.py`), a one-line message in C-25.5's line form and a traceback, stack or message of several lines in a block form that, folded, is the same text; a record that cannot be scrubbed is a fixed `[scrub failed]` line; `daemon.main` sends uncaught, thread and unraisable exceptions, warnings and records no handler takes through the same formatter; each daemon module logs a caught exception only through `refusals.scrubbed_words`; a refused suggestion op logs its own words, scrubbed, before its template replaces them, so C-26.10's daemon.log sentence holds for `suggestion.start`; C-25.7's sentence names exactly which refusals' details `refusals.detail_line` carries.
- C-20.6 (2026-10-20, D-ST1, PR #226 review r2's P3s): no behaviour change. `install_keychain_exec_guard`'s docstring now lists this clause's out-of-scope items in the same words: it adds a shell's script operand, and its symlink item no longer puts every symlink to the reader out of scope, only one that a wrapper's bare command finds through PATH; one launched directly, or given by path as a wrapper's command, is refused by its resolved path and file identity. The clause's own item now says "a symlink to the reader" where it said "one to the reader". A test holds the two lists to the same items. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-19-lsg-p3b.md`.
- C-13.1 (2026-10-20, D-SALV1): concurrent salvage writers that share a ref name re-read it after a create-only collision. The same tree on the same parent reuses the winner; another snapshot goes under the existing `-<tree12>` sibling name, also create-only. A collision there reuses the same tree and parent; any other failure still raises. Base ref naming is unchanged. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-20-salvage-ref-race.md`.
- C-26.4, C-9.2 (2026-10-20, PR #223 round 10): the runner reaches its relay only through `status`, `send` and `close`, or assigns it. Each audited `send` carries an optional per-call `before_send` clock, invoked by the client after connection setup and encoding, immediately before the socket write. The durable stop intent precedes the call; clocks never persist on the relay or run for status, local oversize refusal or failed connection setup. Receipt ordering is unchanged.
- C-17.8, C-17.9 (2026-10-20, hub at #232's round-5 merge): #232's ping-stdin clause is renumbered C-17.9. Release's C-17.8 (`doctor` reports the daemon's environment names outside the allowlist, #222) had been folded into it as a continuation paragraph; it is its own clause again, word for word as release has it.
- C-17.9 (2026-10-20, #232 round 5): restore empty/blank liveness input for finite `TextIOWrapper(BytesIO)` streams, with the same byte cap and replacement decoding; restore release's `parked for <daemon-selected recipient>` message and reason. Arbitrary descriptorless streams still cannot authorize an unbounded read.

- C-5.13 (2026-10-21, #232 review r5, hub fix): a refused connect after a busy answer asks once whether the lock's holder lives, for an opted-out hook client too, as release did and C-16.7 requires; round 5 had taken it as busy until the hook's deadline (up to about 593 s of a 600 s budget for a crashed daemon). C-15.6's opt-out keeps covering a plain refused connect.

- C-5.13 and C-15.6 (2026-10-20, #232 round 5): spend the four-token bucket at actual `ps` starts, after boot reads, so every duration `t` contains at most `4 + t / HOLDER_PROBE_INTERVAL_S` such process starts. Reused verifying clients still recheck identity and stopped state on every send. The hook inspection opt-out also covers a refused connect that follows no busy answer (one after busy asks about the holder, C-16.7); sent unanswered requests retain their C-5.13 diagnosis.

- C-15.2 (2026-10-20, #232 round 5): clarify the six-second hook example: pending gets about 0.25 s after a poll consumes its budget, or about 0.5 s after an immediate terminal reply; marking retains the time left within the original deadline.

- C-12.3 and C-17.9 (2026-10-20, #232 round 5): classify `SUBFLEET_PING_STDIN_WAIT_S` as exact-name configuration, inherited by daemon and provider/helper launches just like `SUBFLEET_HOOK_TIMEOUT_S`.

- C-15.2 (2026-10-19, #232 round 4): a terminal hook poll delivers before idle backoff. Polling reserves up to two seconds, capped at half the post-candidate budget, for pending and mark, and pending may spend at most half the delivery time left.

- C-5.13 (2026-10-19, #232 round 4): one five-second budget covers the whole identity observation, including every boot read and ps. Preflight and failure diagnosis share the call deadline plus five seconds as their absolute ceiling. A four-token bucket replaces completion-based one-second spacing, refilling one token per second while preserving a fresh check before every verifying send.

- C-5.13 and C-17.9 (2026-10-17, #35 port to release/217): diagnose a verified stopped daemon before connecting, preserve unknown outcomes for requests already sent, and fail doctor with the continue command. Bound stdin reads for `ping` and `suggest --brief -` by a total deadline and finite size while retaining release's empty-text health checks.
- C-4.3, C-9.2 (2026-10-20, PR #212 fix round 6): a published receipt establishes an existing copy even when no verdict was saved; crash, ENOSPC, classification and attestation interruptions therefore use the same bounded check retries. Copy cleanup preserves non-regular entries other than symlinks and, after proven invalidity, removes unnamed full and partial copies while preserving evidence named by the receipt or verdict. Exhausted checks report `provider stream copy could not be checked: retry checks exhausted`; a completed check resets the consecutive retry count.

- C-4.3, C-9.2 (2026-10-18, PR #212 fix round 5): an interrupted check of a saved verdict's copy preserves the verdict and retries with worker backoff. Only proven invalidity, or three checks that could not complete in one daemon run, becomes a sticky capture failure; the latter records `copy cannot be checked`. Before a fresh capture with no receipt, abandoned full copies and partial copy temporaries are removed. A legacy cached verdict with no receipt still records a first-capture failure immediately.

- C-20.6 (2026-10-18, D-ST1, PR #226 review r1's P3): a listed wrapper is also judged by the command it runs. A command given by path that is the reader or a registered stand-in is refused, script's `$SHELL -i` counts as its command when it has none, and a command that is itself a listed wrapper is judged again with the PATH, SHELL and directory the outer one leaves, up to 8 listed wrappers deep. An option a wrapper's getopt rejects is refused. stdbuf joins the checked wrappers. `find -exec` stays out of scope because nothing in this repository launches it, not because a check could not tell it from a plain search. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-12-test-live-store-guard.md`.
- C-20.6 (2026-10-18, D-ST1, PR #226 review r1's P2): a direct launch searches PATH only for a bare name, as Popen does, and any launch whose check cannot finish is refused, as a wrapper launch already was. The test safety net does the same. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-12-test-live-store-guard.md`.
- C-20.6 (2026-10-16, D-ST1, PR #198 review round 9's P3s): a wrapper launch whose check cannot finish is refused, not approved; the check reads the working directory only for a relative entry and judges a text and a bytes PATH key both. env's options are walked as its getopt walks them, grouped or not, and `-P`'s path, the child's PATH and `-C`'s directory are all judged; an `env -S` string using `$`, `\` or `#` is refused. `//usr/bin`, `//bin` and `~` entries count. ksh, csh, tcsh, time, script, caffeinate, lockf and arch (with its `-c`, `-d` and `-e`) join the checked wrappers, and the clause names exactly what is checked and what is out of scope. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-12-test-live-store-guard.md`.
- C-5.5 (2026-10-19, D-KILL2 round 2, CI of #234; `docs/reports/2026-10-18-kill2.md`): no behaviour change. The hidden-environment observation now says where it does not hold: GitHub's macos-15-arm64 runner (macOS 15.7.9) returned `/usr/bin/lockf`'s buffer with its environment on 2026-10-19, so there its marker finds it and the incident cannot be reproduced with lockf. Tests of a hidden-marker process run on the host's kernel where it hides the markers, skipping where it shows them, and through a stand-in that hides them on any macOS (`tests/hidden_environment.py`).
- C-5.5 (2026-10-18, D-KILL2; incidents 2026-10-16, `pr219-fix4`'s lockf 14354, and 2026-10-18, `20261018-115510-pr223-fix6`'s lockf 70848; `docs/reports/2026-10-18-kill2.md`): a provider's command ran `/usr/bin/lockf` in a session of its own; the attempt was cancelled and lockf, ppid 1, held the suite lock for up to 1 h 40 min while the installed census (c69757256: group, walk and `ps -axEww` markers) was verified empty. lockf runs as an Apple platform binary with the hardened runtime (its `csops` status), and such a process's argument buffer on macOS 26 holds no environment, so neither `ps -E` nor the raw read sees its markers. Responsibility (D-CEN1) names it while a root lives. Every other member a census counts is now kept as `counted_identities` and seeds later walks, as `responsible_identities` does, so a process counted while its parent lived stays counted after it is reparented, rooted or not; neither grants signal authority. Without a root, a hidden-marker process that leaves the group and walk before any census counts it remains outside every source.
- C-26.4 (2026-10-19, #236 review r2, P3): the cut line is written to the guardian's standard error by a raw write of descriptor 2, no longer logged at warning level through `logging` (logger `subfleet.relay`). A cleanup thread held in that write held `logging`'s handler lock, which `logging.shutdown` takes at exit, so a guardian whose stderr nobody read published its receipt and then stayed alive until the write let go. The cleanup thread now takes no lock that interpreter shutdown takes. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-19-relay-barrier.md`, round 3.
- C-26.4 (2026-10-19, #236 review r2, P2): when relay shutdown's cleanup thread cannot start, shutdown no longer runs the whole cleanup inline, unbounded. It runs only the steps measured not to wait (the listening socket's close, and the pipe's close when no write holds the pipe lock) and returns within the same three-second bound. The cut line is not logged, a pipe that a write still holds is closed by the guardian's exit, and the socket file is left for the daemon's finalization, which already unlinks a turn's relay socket; nothing listens on it. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-19-relay-barrier.md`, round 3.
- C-26.4 (2026-10-19, #236 review r1, P2): relay shutdown is bounded as a whole. After `REPLY_TIMEOUT_S` for the exchange, it waits `JOIN_TIMEOUT_S` (one second, the join budget that was a literal) for the relay thread and `CLEANUP_TIMEOUT_S` (one second) for a cleanup thread that closes the socket and the pipe, logs the cut and unlinks the socket file: three seconds in all. `stdin.jsonl` records are written and fsynced under a journal lock of their own, held for the whole frame, never under the pipe lock, so a stalled record write or fsync no longer holds the pipe's close or `exit.json`. Record order and durability are unchanged, and `status` still waits for a frame being applied. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-19-relay-barrier.md`, round 2.
- C-26.4 (2026-10-19, #230 review r1, P3): relay shutdown's wait for an exchange already read is bounded by `REPLY_TIMEOUT_S` (one second), the bound replies already had. An exchange still going then is cut: both halves of its connection are shut, a stdin write waiting on a pipe nobody reads (the write end is now non-blocking) is logged `failed` with `ECANCELED`, one fixed line is logged, and shutdown goes on. A process that keeps a finished child's stdin open without reading no longer holds `exit.json`. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-19-relay-barrier.md`.
- C-6.9, C-6.10, C-6.11 (2026-10-19, D-ADM3; `docs/reports/2026-10-19-admission-adm3.md`): a job started by this admission pass no longer blocks as an older waiter. A backed-off `behind-older-job` hold is reused only while its named predecessor remains a capacity waiter in the current pass; otherwise the job is looked at immediately, with all current caps and older waiters still applying. This also covers a predecessor that ended, became unadmittable, or now waits for a lease.

- C-9.2 (2026-10-19, #223 fix round 9): every conversation frame explicitly declares `stops`, and the runner audits that flag before transmission. This includes Claude approval replies with `interrupt:true`, Codex approval `cancel` decisions and legacy `abort` decisions. Tool-only denial, allow/answer replies, queued-message cancellation and elicitation cancellation do not stop the active turn. Suppressing a redundant interrupt after approval cancellation preserves the wall escalation and the same conservative receipt-ordering rules.

- C-7.3, C-9.2 (2026-10-19, #223 fix round 8): client and operator cancels cascade in the request transaction; a wall cancel cascades only in the parent's interrupted settlement transaction, preserving receipt-wins parents and known-exited children. Provider interrupts, every relay signal and stdin close have durable pre-transmission `attempt.stop_delivery` intents alongside the kill protocol's `attempt.kill_started`; only an exit strictly before every delivery can supersede a wall cancel. Pending or unclocked intents and timestamp ties remain conservative across restart.

- C-4.2, C-4.5, C-5.12, C-6.4, C-7.3, C-11.8, C-17.1, C-17.4 (2026-10-14, revised 2026-10-15, D-WALL1 and D-KILL1): `max_wall_s` bounds summed provider running time through starting and running, ending at a known provider exit. Finalization and settlement delays spend none of it; an exited provider keeps its own outcome, with an overrun recorded when the daemon missed the deadline. Queues, capacity waits, operator holds and gaps between attempts spend none of it; a job without an executing attempt is never cancelled by the wall. Every cancellation records its source and reason; client kills carry the requesting session and socket peer pid, accept `--reason`, and notify another session's caller. `runs show`, including offline reads and `--json`, labels the session as client-stated and the pid as socket-derived. A pending wall stop may be superseded by a receipt proving exit before the signal; a client request takes precedence, and the wall cascades only after the parent's interrupted settlement. An attempt signalled before provider exit never becomes `ok`, even with `turn.completed`; equal timestamps and second-precision ties are conservative (C-9.2, D-WALL1). Receipt-less attempts are inspected before the wall: a dead provider settles lost and its charge ends at its last observed liveness, excluding host or daemon downtime; only a provider confirmed alive can be wall-stopped. Unpinned retries every candidate refuses permanently have C-11.8's notice and grace; operator holds and provider limits with a reset remain unbounded. Conversation client stops carry client attribution, and repeated kills still reach live dependent descendants. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-14-wall-running-time.md`.
- C-23.14 (2026-10-18, D-SCR1, #225 review r2): the `Bearer` and header rules never take a credential key a named-value rule would read. A `Bearer` token that begins with such a key and its separator, or with a flag and its value, is not a token; a header leaves the key that ends its line, when that key's value is past the line, to the named-value rules and redacts what precedes it. Ordinary headers are redacted and counted as before; a key shaped like a JWT, a prefixed token or a long base64 run is still read as a token.
- C-1.3, C-10.5, C-11.1, C-11.8, C-12.3, C-20.6 (2026-10-18, D-ENV1 round 5, review r4 of #222): round 4 seeded `lanes.json` before checking it, so a refused lane was stored, and the stored lane then refused every later start, by launchd's daemon and `daemon start` alike, with no supported way out. The check now runs before seeding, a stored lane with a variable every launch inherits is held (`credential-refused`) rather than fatal, and a fixed `lanes.json` row repairs it in place. Both refusals name the lane, where it came from and the variable. Selection skips a name popped between listing and reading, rejects a withheld name before reading its value, and selects a test session's names only beside the session marker.
- C-10.5, C-11.1, C-12.3, C-17.8, C-20.6 (2026-10-16, D-ENV1 round 4, #222 with #198 and review r3): D-ENV1's allowlist dropped D-ST1's session markers and isolated paths, so a captured test child lost its isolation. A live test session's own names are now selected by exact name while the parent is in a live session as `home_paths.under_pytest` decides it (the marker with a live sentinel, a bare marker, or `PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST`; round 5 drops the last), and never otherwise; liveness is read from the session probe's own names only, so selection still reads no rejected value. An env-kind lane whose variable some launch inherits is refused at daemon start and startup, which exits 2; doctor lists the lanes' variables on their own row.
- C-10.5, C-11.1, C-12.3, C-17.8 (2026-10-16, D-ENV1 round 3, review r2 of #222): the `SUBFLEET_` prefix rule admitted credentials whose words it matched only whole (`SUBFLEET_TEST_OAUTH`, the repo's own env-kind example, reached every lane). Subfleet configuration is now exact names, with the harness's own prefixes and a substring credential-word backstop; an env-kind lane's credential reaches only its own lane, `env.pass` may not name one, and doctor names every dropped name.
- C-11.1, C-11.2, C-12.3/4, C-15.1 and C-17.2 (2026-10-14, D-SH1 round 2, hub design after Fleet ops 11:27Z/11:33Z): Codex read-only launches run commands but cannot write even temporary files. Shipped reviews now use conditional `worktree-write`, and shell is opt-in. Attempts record `none`, `commands` or `writable`; notices, runs show and last-24-hour status expose restricted runs. Review tasks and gate rounds deny git push and gh writes through launch restrictions shared by both providers; deliverables remain untouched. This supersedes the round-1 default below.
- C-11.1, C-11.2, C-12.4, C-15.1 and C-17.2 (2026-10-14, D-SH1, microcosm-dynamics coordinator report measured by the hub): 466 read-only Claude reviews succeeded during 2026-10-07–14; 314 prompts asked for commands and 407 deliverables reported no shell. Track B reviews `20261014-043440-trackb-d1408-review3b` and `20261014-050611-trackb-g-review5` requested changes solely because tests could not run. Shell is now a persisted job requirement: incompatible launches are removed from candidates, unsatisfiable pins fail at submit, and capacity waits explain the requirement. CLI and batch overrides supersede policy `requires`; shipped reviews require shell. Attempts, show JSON and completion notices report shell capability without changing deliverables. Model pins are validated by the daemon against its policy; CLI accepts `sol61`, `luna` and future policy models, while retaining the `sol` retirement note. Live policy installation belongs to the hub after Fleet ops is told; this change edits only the shipped example.
- C-11.1, C-12.3, C-12.4, C-17.8 (2026-10-14, D-ENV1, reported by d1063 and verified by the hub): a shell-started daemon inherited unrelated exported credentials and passed them into every lane and provider tool. Daemon startup and provider/helper launches now select an environment allowlist before adding launch-owned variables; policy `env.pass` authorizes exact extra names. The guardian selects again, and doctor reports extra daemon names without values. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-14-launch-env-allowlist.md`.
- CI flakes (2026-10-17, D-2 and D-RL1): C-5.8a uses a managed Python thread snapshot at the drain deadline, with the C dumper halfway through the existing three-second margin and the kernel alarm at its end; the 30-second grace, 33-second kernel bound and 40-second external backstops are unchanged. C-26.4 makes child-exit shutdown finish an exchange already read before publishing the exit receipt, while idle reads are woken immediately and reply writes have a one-second bound.

- C-29.7, C-27.5 (2026-10-14, Max's 2026-09-28 ask): messages waiting behind a turn, a block or an earlier queued message sit in a compact tray above the composer, with Withdraw and an eligible head's Send now. An idle send stays in the timeline across its queued receipt; pre-send withdrawals, including tombstones, leave one line. Conversations open at the bottom without an animated scroll.
- C-4.3, C-8.2, C-26.8 (2026-10-16, PR #212 fix round 4): capture-failure verdicts are durable across replay and restart. A valid recorded copy and its verdict are reused without another capture; missing, replaced, changed or newly writable recorded evidence becomes a sticky capture failure, never a fresh read of the live candidate. Conversation turns are exempt from stream capture: their driver's `turn.json` outcome decides settlement. Rejected copies are removed before retry or capture failure. A legacy receipt that replaced its copy cannot lend that copy to an unbound cached verdict. Only this PR's unmerged rounds wrote the `reset_finalization` marker.

- C-4.3, C-8.2 (2026-10-15, PR #212 fix round 3): failed capture settles in the same pass as `transient` for Codex or `unknown` for other provider attempts, with best-effort diagnostics. Finalized readers use the recorded `raw-stream` artifact; capture need not create `stream.jsonl`.

- C-4.3, C-8.2, C-9.2, C-12.6 (2026-10-14, PR #212 fix round 2): descriptor identity replaces path names and link counts as the authority test. Every regular candidate is copied in bounded chunks to a fresh daemon-owned inode, with a durable receipt identifying its path, device/inode, size and SHA-256. Replay validates and uses that exact copy; it never re-copies an original provider path while retaining cached classification or extraction. Losing or changing the copy required both to run from scratch (superseded by fix round 4's capture failure). Mode-000 non-regular replacements are distinguished from genuine regular-file permission errors, and post-check ENOENT on a still-present regular file propagates.

- C-4.3, C-8.2, C-9.2, C-12.6 (2026-10-14, PR #209 review follow-ups, first implementation superseded by fix round 2): finalization detaches every multiply linked regular stream with write-then-rename and captures a raw path naming live stdout at the canonical path. The conservative `st_nlink > 1` rule covers writable aliases without a provider-specific artifact list; later writes cannot add completion proof or change extracted text. Artifact capture records the authoritative regular stream, including canonical `stream.jsonl` beside an absent custom raw path. A non-regular replacement during open supplies no events: `ENXIO`, `EOPNOTSUPP` (102 on Darwin), `ENOTSUP`, `ELOOP`, `EISDIR` and `EINVAL` become `NotRegularFile`; other I/O errors still propagate.
- C-23.14, C-25.7 and C-26.10 (2026-10-16, #225 review r1): a secret assignment's key may be quoted with escaped closing quotes (a repr, or JSON encoded more than once); a repr-escaped value closes at its matching escaped quote and keeps the rest of its line; excerpts merge overlapping assignments as whole-text scrubs do; the daemon.log sentence covers refusal details written through `refusals.detail_line`, not every daemon.log writer; `conversation.create` answers an unreadable protected-path spelling with `workspace.check`'s template.
- C-25.7, C-29.14, C-26.10, C-14.2 and C-23.14 (2026-10-15, #181 review r8): secret-shaped folder refusals explain the path restriction without echoing it; unreadable protected-path spellings retain the searchable-directory repair; guard repairs follow the checked verdict kind, with trust pins named only for trust failures; person-only start and dismiss refusals leave suggestions open, including reparented callers with terminals; local daemon log scrubbing also removes repr-escaped and unterminated quoted assignment values.
- C-6.9, C-9.5 (2026-10-16, D-CAP1, #207 review r4c, fix round 5): a steered waiter is judged for standing refusal on its whole original chain, including the steered models, both on its clock and when due or after exhausted route retries. A bounded capacity steer cannot make a temporary wait permanently unadmittable.
- C-9.5, C-4.5, C-11.2 (2026-10-14, D-CAP1 review round 2, #207): live capacity steers exclude the refused model from admission. With no admissible unsteered model, the job waits for capacity, names the steer and its end in `why`, and rechecks by expiry. Capacity classification accepts only provider error messages and top-level provider stderr errors, excluding tool diagnostics and quoted payloads. Pinned retries keep their existing backoff.
- C-9.5, C-4.5, C-11.2 (2026-10-13, D-CAP1; jobs `20261013-024327-pr198-review-r6`, `20261013-015056-pr181-fix8` and `hub-fix-github-125-r7-resume`, submitted as `20261013-030841-…` and again as `20261013-040242-…`; `docs/reports/2026-10-13-model-capacity-steer.md`): Codex's "Selected model is at capacity. Please try a different model." ended 84 of 5,507 Codex attempts from 2026-10-01 to 2026-10-13 (82 on gpt-6.1-sol), after a median 17 minutes of work, and each retry asked the same model again, the first on the same lane 60 s later. The refusal stays `transient` with no closure and records `model_capacity`, with the refusal, not an earlier recovered reconnect, as its admission evidence. On a job with no model or lane pin that is no turn, resume or revive, the attempt records `capacity_steer` (the model, until `MODEL_CAPACITY_STEER_S`, 900 s, after it finished), sets no same-lane retry and leaves the job due at the next pass, and until then admission skips that model for the job: the first admissible unsteered model in chain order takes the job, or it waits for capacity until a candidate becomes available or the steer ends. It is the job's alone, because the model served 350 of the 390 attempts started within 15 minutes of a refusal; no lane, account or other job is touched. A job pinned to the model or a lane keeps the same-lane retry after 60 s.
- C-20.6, C-23.28 (2026-10-15, D-ST1, PR #198 round 8): define the test harness boundary and exclusions; keep an isolated session HOME and persistent safe path overrides, fold bare reader names, and refuse shell/env-family launches whose child PATH reaches a system binary directory before the recorder. Keep the original uv cache and read account-home non-store inputs through `pwd`, so HOME isolation does not silently drop coverage. The filesystem trap also guards firmlink spellings, `io.FileIO` and `os.chdir`. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-12-test-live-store-guard.md`.
- C-5.1, C-5.5, C-5.12 (2026-10-16, PR #220 fix rounds 2–4): a responsibility root without a recorded unique id needs a matching live snapshot and one fresh start-time read before the member scan. After every applicable scan, for either kind of root, one in-process kernel read checks the current holder. A readable successor unique id excludes only members born at or after that successor; real members observed before root death are retained. An absent or unreadable holder, or a successor without readable ordering, preserves collected provisional evidence with an unverifiable result. A known-id root already gone with no provisional members can still verify empty. A changed start on a root without a recorded unique id also reports the identity diagnostic. Member live state and birth identities come from the same kernel records as their unique ids, so an older snapshot holder or a later pid holder cannot replace that observation. Different ownership and responsibility births at one pid remain alternative census seeds; pruning requires every recorded birth to be provably different. Same-second reuse and a root dead before a member read remain C-5.3/C-5.5 limits; missing successor ordering may conservatively retain strangers. See the round-4 section of `docs/reports/2026-10-14-census-responsibility-p3.md`.

- C-5.1, C-5.5, C-5.9 (2026-10-14, PR #205 review r2, P3-A and P3-B): a responsibility root recorded without a unique id requires a live snapshot row with its recorded start time; otherwise its source contributes no members and records an identity diagnostic, so a new pid holder cannot save strangers as walk seeds. See `docs/reports/2026-10-14-census-responsibility-p3.md`. C-5.9 points to C-5.2's responsibility wait after the exit receipt. C-5.5's residual escape names a process a member starts with responsibility disclaimed; a running member cannot disclaim itself.
- C-20.6, C-23.28 (2026-10-15, D-ST1, PR #198 round 8): define the test harness boundary and exclusions; keep an isolated session HOME and persistent safe path overrides, fold bare reader names, and refuse shell/env-family launches whose child PATH reaches a system binary directory before the recorder. Keep the original uv cache and read account-home non-store inputs through `pwd`, so HOME isolation does not silently drop coverage. The filesystem trap also guards firmlink spellings, `io.FileIO` and `os.chdir`. Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-12-test-live-store-guard.md`.
- C-25.7, C-29.14 (2026-10-11, review r1b of #181): a suggestion-started conversation takes no message but its brief until the brief is accepted, refused in each message's own transaction (exit 7 `conversation-starting`); the first message's id is random, chosen with the claim and private until accepted; Start only uses the conversation its claim wrote, which has no request id, and `suggestion:` request ids are refused to create and handoff (exit 2 `reserved-request-id`); every suggestion error is scrubbed in full before whitespace folding, the folded form scrubbed again, then cut without splitting markers to at most 500 characters before storage, logging or return (including response repair text and local CLI notices), a folder path, session or calling job the scrubber would change is refused, and no cut of a title, summary, error, verdict or listed brief splits what scrubbing put in (finishing round: 6bdb6fee5, d59808686); where a suggestion starts (`workspace_kind`, `repository`) is decided when it is made, shown on its card, and what Start does; `suggestion.list` carries `watch_seq`, and the app keeps any suggestion the feed delivered after an answer was read; the daemon's retries of a claimed start wait 2, 4, 8, ... s as stated.
- C-25.7, C-29.14, with C-25.2, C-25.3 and C-25.6 (2026-10-11, suggested conversations): an agent proposes a separate conversation with `subfleet suggest`; the app shows it as a card in the conversation that proposed it and in the sidebar's Suggested section; a person starts it (a conversation made as `conversation.create` makes one, a worktree conversation when asked for in a git checkout, with the brief as its first message) or dismisses it, and its creator may withdraw it while it is open. `starting` is the person's claim, taken in the transaction that writes the new conversation's row; the brief is accepted in the transaction that records `started`. The suggestions table is additive and the conversation store stays schema 2; `conversation_schema` stays 1 (the ops are a capability, `suggestions.v1`, and a watch row's two new fields are optional).
- C-5.1, C-5.2, C-5.5, C-5.12 (2026-10-13, D-CEN1): attempt `20261013-015921-pr213-suite-rerun-inplace/a1` left detached vitest 19935 and worker 21910 invisible after node rewrote their titles; only esbuild 21192's marker prevented release. The marker source now reads raw `KERN_PROCARGS2` buffers, and guardians launched by a daemon verified self-responsible at startup are responsibility roots whose lineage supplies a fourth source even when a process erases its environment. A rooted guardian stays after its exit receipt while descendants name it; discovered responsibility members remain census seeds after the root dies, with no new signal authority. Legacy attempts keep three sources. The deciding census costs one process-table `ps`, one additional early start-time `ps` for an otherwise matching root without a recorded unique id, an in-process pid listing and per-candidate kernel reads, plus one in-process root-holder read after each applicable responsibility scan. The final holder read bounds strangers by a readable successor's birth order and preserves previously observed real members when the root dies; unavailable ordering retains collected provisional evidence with an unverifiable result; a known-id root already gone without provisional members can still verify empty (C-5.5). See `docs/reports/2026-10-13-census-responsibility.md`.
- C-6.9, C-6.11, C-6.12 (2026-10-12, stateful admission properties, findings 5 and 6, `docs/reports/2026-10-12-admission-properties.md`): a job held behind an older one while its capacity clock runs still holds back the later jobs of its tier that compete with it, with the demand it has while that clock runs (a pinned retry's pair, or its own once a look has let the pin go). Before, it registered as no waiter, so in a pool with a count cap a younger job that competed with it and not with the older job was placed past it. A lease wait still holds nobody, nor does a job no lane can admit, whose `behind-older-job` hold now carries `for_good` (a lease waiter's too). Admission asks C-11.8 of such a job, and of any job on its clock, with the lanes its attempts excluded merged in as its route merges them (C-4.5): a lane re-enrolled since a `limited` attempt is excluded under its successor's id, which the stored exclusions do not name (review of #201 r1). A job whose exclusions cannot be merged (a stored one that is not a name) holds nobody, as the `route` wait its route's preparation settles it into holds nobody; a retry whose pin a look let go is settled there rather than held behind an older job (review of #201 r2). The added holds last only while that job's clock runs: held behind, it gets no look and so no new clock. A transient retry whose pin was let go is the exception C-6.12 already makes: its due look can let the pin go again and set a new clock, so it holds its younger competitors for as long as the older job waits and its pair stays refused.
- C-9.6 (2026-10-13, defect D-HOLD1): an operator hold keeps its own closure row. `Store.put_closure` kept one open closure per lane and scope, so a weekly limit reported on a held lane (by an attempt that was already running) rewrote the hold's row as its own; the usage read that later saw the window reset released it, and the lane was admitted hours before the hold's end. The reverse merge dropped a limit that ended before a longer hold, so `lanes release` opened a lane that was still limited. Holds (`operator-hold`, `auth-dead`) and limits now each keep their own row; the v1 importer's cooldown merge follows the same rule. A hold absorbed before this change stays absorbed: `lanes hold` it again to give it its row.
- C-4.3, C-9.2, C-12.6 (2026-10-13, PR #195 review follow-up, cached blank deliverables): finalization rechecks captured content on every replay and restart using the fresh provider boundary (ASCII whitespace for Codex bytes, Unicode whitespace for Claude text), preserving payload bytes. A cached `ok` with a blank result is not accepted and becomes `unknown`, `empty deliverable with rc 0`, under the existing empty-result rule; a one-attempt job fails without exporting it.

- C-4.3, C-9.2 (2026-10-13, PR #195 review follow-up, Claude frozen streams): Claude's diagnostic text and full-summary readers use the same frozen-entry authority as Codex. An empty, whitespace-only or malformed frozen file cannot expose a later stdout result, including recovery interrupted before classification or deliverable capture.
- C-4.3, C-9.2 (2026-10-13, PR #195 review follow-up, non-regular frozen streams): every existing frozen-path object is authoritative. Empty regular files mean no events; directories, FIFOs and symlinks mean no events and are neither followed nor read. Freezing preserves those objects at either frozen candidate, including a canonical entry beside an absent custom launch path, and only absent frozen paths permit legacy stdout fallback.

- C-6.1, C-6.12 (2026-10-12, stateful admission properties, `docs/reports/2026-10-12-admission-properties.md`): exclusions are names, and merging them with a retry's is part of evaluating the route. Code brought to the text of C-4.1, C-6.9, C-6.10 and C-6.11: a lane-pinned task job competes on the one model it walks; a capacity wait's verdict is `scheduler.verdict_signature` alone, not whether a capped pool was at its limit; a job whose probe was quarantined on this pass is `uncertain` and holds nobody.
- C-25.6 (2026-10-11, defect D-PB1; Max agreed in d1350 that it is fixed first): a controlling terminal no longer admits a caller to the person-only operations. Only the installed app's executable does, or on a development state root the development build named when the daemon started (D-21), after the guardian and marker vetoes, which are unchanged. Before, an agent-started process that had dropped its markers and kept a pseudo-terminal passed as the person once launchd adopted it, and so did a terminal child it forked and any SSH login. Refusing only a caller whose parent is launchd would not have closed it. No `subfleet` subcommand sends a person-only operation, so the CLI is unchanged; the end-to-end tests, which sent them through `script(1)`'s terminal, now send them as a development-app stand-in (`tests/e2e/dev_app.py`) and check that the terminal is refused. The clause also names `conversation.create` and `conversation.handoff` above Ask or on main, which the code already checked (IR-21). Report: `docs/reports/2026-10-11-person-boundary.md`.
- C-6.18 (2026-10-10, detached placement ramp, D-MG1): opt-in `admission.ramp` bounds detached placements by recent attempt reservations and counts this pass immediately, including retries and priority starts, so reopening disk or machine gates and restarting the daemon grant no fresh burst. Priority callers bypass its hold; attended turns and probes remain exempt. The loader refuses a rate and window whose allowance per window is fractional.
- C-8.4, C-8.5, C-6.5 (2026-10-10, #141 fix round 2; review of 2cbf6fca, P2-A and P2-B, and round 1's P3s): an `unspelled` turn's Git folder is found again before the look for retention's fence, and the look and the reserving transaction use that one answer, so a persisted `core.worktree` naming a fenced top elsewhere holds the turn before its workspace is prepared; the lookup is a read-only `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`. A push job's validated top is never `unspelled` and never found again with Git at admission, so its write target stays the checkout it validated. C-8.4 now says what the look's hold shares with the transaction's when another blocker is present, and its known limit for `worktree-in-use` covers every name of the tree's path, the folders above the tree included.
- 2026-10-11 (PR #185, fix round 1): C-5.3/C-5.5 retain recorded escaped survivors through absent, zombie and recycled roots, treat incomparable identities as unknown, remove wall-clock bounds as death evidence, and correct the XNU citation.

- C-5.5 (2026-10-11, recycled-PID incident: qyp16-r1c child 4203, pb-kit-u4-docs leader 243, pe-uk-2192-r2/a2 child 16379): every deciding census receives recorded root identities through `_contain`; proven recycled roots and groups are excluded, missing identities retain observed live processes as unverifiable, uncertain legacy identities retain containment, and excluded identities are recorded without commands or environments (`docs/reports/2026-10-11-census-recycled-pid.md`).
- C-8.5 (2026-10-10, #158 fix round 4): object intake refuses sparse files and caps bytes, startup clears abandoned quarantines under daemon.lock, config looks share a 256 KiB snapshot, and standalone checkout builds run on the push thread so other admission placements never wait for the build.
- C-9.2, C-12.6 (2026-10-12, PR #195 with release/217's #186): a detached Codex attempt is `ok` only with `turn.completed` in its own stream; an rc-0 attempt without it is `transient` unless authentication, admission or quota evidence answers first. Where the two rules met, #186's stands: a completed turn with a nonblank deliverable is verified success, and recovered authentication, admission or quota text does not undo it (this PR had ranked a recovered authentication message above it).
- C-9.2, C-12.6, C-17.1 (2026-10-13, PR #195 fix round 4, its first full CI run): a verdict this daemon classified carries `rule` in `finalization.json`, and every replay keeps it (C-4.3), so a transient attempt whose salvage timed out (C-13.1) still retries; only an older daemon's cached verdict is reconciled with completion proof. The bare `runs show` keeps v1's shape, with nothing after `--- out.md ---` when no result was accepted and the unaccepted evidence named on stderr; `--out` keeps `no deliverable recorded`; an accepted job with no deliverable recorded for any attempt shows its `-o` export again (C-8.3) unless that export failed. Test fixtures that model Codex lanes (host push, settle, gate peer) carry `turn.completed`.
- C-9.2, C-9.8 and C-12.6 (2026-10-11, PR #186 fix round 1): restore both adapters' nonblank-deliverable checks and the Codex success detail. Removing a deliverable can remove acceptance; the model's wording remains excluded from all provider failure signatures and closure clocks. A completion frame alone is insufficient.

- C-9.2–C-9.8 and C-12.6 (2026-10-11, model-text limit incident, 12:30Z–13:23Z): seven successful Claude Opus 5.5 attempts quoted usage-limit phrases in reports, failing three jobs and closing five accounts for an hour. All text classification and closure clocks now use CLI/provider evidence only. Successful completion supersedes text failures; a structured Claude admission rejection remains authoritative. Model wording changes that preserve deliverable presence preserve the adapter verdict and closure; adapters and finalization reject empty deliverables. Operators can release guessed provider-limit/credits closures with `lanes release <lane> --limits --note <text>`, with reported clocks preserved and an audit event.

- C-8.5 (2026-10-10, #158 fix round 3): no push job's workspace stops an admission pass. A refusal from the checkout or Git fails that job with exit 7 and a notice; the host's own trouble while a job's checkout is copied goes to C-6.8's workspace handling; the standalone checkout is renamed into place whole. Submit refuses a shallow checkout, and, for a job not in place, one that borrows objects (alternates) or is a partial clone. A symlink's `.git` component is also matched without what HFS+ ignores in names and in Git's NTFS spellings.
- C-8.5 (2026-10-10, merge of release/217 at cf3a22e2 into #158): a push job's export, once its push settles, follows C-8.3 and C-6.5 as any job's, pushed or refused; its leases stay held until then. A push job without an accepted attempt hands nothing to the push thread.
- C-8.5 (2026-10-09, Max d1098 option A; #158 fix round 1): an explicitly opted-in dispatch may publish its accepted workspace bundle through the host's existing Git credential helper. Push policy ships disabled; safe descriptor-based intake, normalized casefolded branch protection and ownership, strict symlink rules, bounded quarantine verification, restart markers, a dedicated push thread, and capability-gated clients enforce the contract. Fix round 2: checkout metadata is read only as capped regular files (a FIFO had held submit), and symlink targets with a `.git` component are refused. Push never forces or deletes; failures preserve acceptance and audit survives retirement. Existing CI and preview deployments still expose the changed code to repository secrets, as the clause records.
- C-6.11 (2026-10-08, PR #148 CI fixes): output keys in holds and lease queues use C-6.5's comparison identity even when a legacy raw alias supplies the guard. Recorded output paths retain their display spelling.
- C-6.5, C-26.3, C-26.13 (2026-10-06, canonical resource identity; audit L1–L3): output ownership compares resolved folder identity followed by canonical caseless matching on case-insensitive volumes, including absent destinations and parent aliases; native UUID ownership compares canonical lowercase UUIDs. These keys govern submit, reservation, export and recovery while display spellings remain unchanged. Older raw lease spellings remain guards through alias-aware lookup, including multiple old holders. Resume and revive re-read conversation ownership inside the reservation transaction, after preparation and before leases or attempts.
- C-11.1, C-11.2 (2026-10-09, tier preferences): each task chain has one entry per tier, either a short model name or a non-empty list of distinct current model names in preference order. Routing flattens only the entries from the job's tier upward, removes duplicates keeping the first occurrence, and tries each model in order. Within a tier, a later model is tried only when no lane of an earlier model admits the job; order across tiers is unchanged. `fallback: upward-only` still excludes entries below the job's tier. Existing string-only policies retain their routing behavior; the shipped default chains are unchanged.
- C-6.9, C-6.10, C-6.11, C-6.3, C-11.4 (2026-10-08, defect D-1; incident 2026-10-03 18:36Z to 19:40Z, `docs/reports/2026-10-08-probe-vehicle-starvation.md`): a `hard` review drew four admission probes that ran to their 60 s deadline and read `unknown` or `transient`; each time it waited a minute and then a whole pass, 10 to 21 minutes, while later hard reviews probed the same Codex lanes and started. A probe says nothing about the job that carries it, so the jobs waiting on one now form a line in the pass's order and the first that could use a probe carries it (C-6.9); a job whose probe said nothing tries the model's other lanes before its first again (C-11.4); its clock runs from the probe's reservation, so a probe its deadline stopped leaves it due at once (C-6.10); `why` names the probe and whose turn it is (C-6.11); and a probe stopped at its deadline says so in its evidence (C-11.4).
- C-7.4 (2026-10-06, invariants audit A1): admission probe reservation and gate decisions re-read cancellation and terminal state inside their Store transactions; a refused gate contains the probe and releases its lease only after verified containment.
- C-6.16 (2026-10-06, Max: "give axiom promise maximum priority"): `admission.priority_callers` selects caller sessions case-insensitively, including their descendants. Their detached jobs sort ahead of session and background work, FIFO across tiers, and bypass the machine guard. `why` names the class and policy key; `status` names the configured callers from the registry when available. The default is null.
- C-11.9 (2026-10-06): human status and `status.json` show projected unused weekly quota per lane and scope from the provider's own readings, with an explicit unknown rate and provider totals. These forecasts are projections, not promises; admission does not use them.
- C-8.4 (2026-10-06, the 2.1.11 cut): with `SUBFLEET_RETENTION_DORMANT=1` in the daemon's environment, a retention pass prunes delivered service notices and nothing else. It selects, archives and deletes no job, marks the timer without an error, and logs once per daemon that retention is dormant. Any other value, or none, leaves retention as specified. 2.1.11's installer sets it in the launchd plist, not in `policy.json` (d574), because retention's fences still match a job's own tree exactly (design `docs/desktop/retention-archive.md` §15). The 2.1.12 install removes it once the fence fixes stacked on #134 land.
- C-8.4, C-13.4 (2026-10-05, shared folders on 2.1.11): port I5 to the retention-by-archive driver. Its pin census and archive commit recheck protect `folders.TURN` and `folders.READER` rows as `turn-folder`; its selecting transaction checks those rows again before taking the exclusive retirement fence. Admission checks that fence before reserving a writer or reader. The fence and archive journal use the same canonical spelling, established outside the store transaction. The archive, verification, byte-signature reclaim and registration-preservation rules remain binding. The historical shared-folder note about not-yet-started jobs is superseded by #76's `worktree-in-use` pin for recorded queued jobs.
- Review of the I5 port (599af189, 2026-10-05, P3-1 and P3-2; with the review of 31048e67, F1): C-8.4, a turn row on a folder inside a job's worktree, such as a conversation in a repository nested there, keeps the job in the pin census, the selecting transaction and the commit recheck; before, only a row on the worktree itself did, so once that turn's attempt was quarantined (its job `lost`, its row kept) nothing kept the tree. The commit also reads the rows on the journal's canonical spelling, as the selecting transaction does, so a recorded worktree in another case or an unrecorded one is kept too. The `turn-folder` pin now keeps only jobs with their own allocated worktree: an in-place job's folder is never removed, so holding those jobs kept only their rows and job directories, which no live turn needs, and stopped the turn and detached pools shrinking while a conversation was active. Turn reservation still fences only a turn's own folder (known limit, above).
- A turn in a job directory (2026-10-09, PR #138 review 3): C-8.4, retirement keeps `jobs/<job>` while any live or queued turn's actual cwd is that directory or inside it, even when its Git hold is elsewhere. Before quarantine, it atomically rechecks turn rows and recorded turn cwd paths and takes a canonical job-directory fence, including on recovery. Later turns wait on that fence; a queued turn keeps the job at the archive commit, rolls retirement back and starts after the fence is released.
- A turn's actual cwd (2026-10-09, PR #138 review 3): C-8.4, C-24.5, turn reservation checks retention fences on the canonical folder the provider runs in as well as its write target or reader hold, and on every folder above each. Persisted Git `core.worktree` may put the hold outside the cwd. Canonicalization stays outside the reserving transaction. When cwd and hold differ, an additional reader row keeps the cwd in place without changing the Git hold's exact-folder writer rules. The wait reason names the fenced folder, including an exact-folder fence.
- Admission of a turn nested in a tree being retired (2026-10-05, the known limit the review of the I5 port left; probe in #134's evidence): C-8.4, C-24.5, a turn, writable or read-only, in a repository nested in a job's worktree was reserved while retention held that tree's fence, because reservation read the fence only on the turn's own folder, the nested repository's top level. The commit then rolled the retirement back, but the quarantine had already moved the tree from under the live turn. Reservation now reads the fence on every folder above the turn's too, in the same transaction and by string operations (`folders.above`, `folders.retiring`). The turn waits `lease-held` through the retirement, and its queued job keeps the tree at the commit (`worktree-in-use`). C-6.11, C-24.4: a turn's lease hold names its folder, so its message says retention is removing the tree that folder is in. C-6.5 is unchanged: a detached writer's lease on a folder above holds no turn.
- Review of 31048e67 (F3, 2026-10-05): C-8.4, a `worktree:` lease on a folder inside a job's own allocated worktree, held by anything but that job's retirement, keeps the job as `worktree-lease`, in the pin census (recorded spellings), the selecting transaction and the commit recheck (the canonical spelling), as a turn row there does. Before, `worktree-lease` matched the worktree itself only, and the selecting transaction checked only `worktree:<worktree>`, so once a detached in-place writer in a repository nested in the tree had its attempt quarantined (its job `lost`, its lease kept), nothing kept the job, and retention archived it and deleted its tree under the writer's folder. The leases are read by one key range (`worktree:<folder>/` up to `worktree:<folder>0`), never `LIKE`, which ignores ASCII case and reads `_` and `%` as wildcards. At that review, detached writer reservation fenced only the writer's own folder; #140 extends it to every folder above its write target, as turn reservation does.
- Admission of a detached writer nested in a tree being retired (2026-10-05, the writer half of the known limit the review of 31048e67 left, F3; probe in #137's evidence): C-8.4, C-6.5, a detached writer in place in a repository nested in a job's worktree holds `worktree:<that repository>`, and reservation read retention's fence only on that key, so a writer submitted after selection was reserved while retention held the tree's fence (`'live': True` under `'fence': 'retention:retired'`). While the writer's job was active the commit then rolled the retirement back (`worktree-in-use`; once its attempt was quarantined, only #137's `worktree-lease` on a folder inside the tree does), but the quarantine had already moved the tree from under the reserved writer. Writer reservation now reads the fence on every folder above its write target too, as a turn's does (`folders.retiring`, in the same transaction, string operations only). The writer waits `lease-held` through the retirement, its hold naming the fence's key (C-6.11, `why`), and runs on the first detached pass after the fence goes (C-6.10). Only retention's fence above counts: a detached writer in place in the checkout around a nested repository, a writable turn's row there, or any other holder of `worktree:` above holds no writer in it (C-6.5). Submission accepts such a writer, its own key being free, and it waits. Review of 5253faa2: P2, submission kept a job's workdir as typed (`resolve`d only), and `worktree-in-use` compares recorded strings in SQL (`=` compares case, LIKE folds ASCII only), so a writer whose workdir was typed in NFD, with a non-ASCII name in other capitals, on the tree itself in other capitals, or through the `/System/Volumes/Data` firmlink, kept no tree, and the retirement deleted the tree it waited to write in; so did a turn, writable or read-only, through the firmlink (the case-alias review's P3, chip task_4972080c), or on the tree itself in other capitals (the second sentence of C-8.4's known limit, now narrowed to a folder whose names submission cannot all look up); submission now records the workdir in its one spelling (`folders.canonical`, as far as each name can be looked up then; the payload digest keeps the resolved path, so a request submitted again is the same payload). P3, a lease-held job looked at on its clock re-queued every key of its hold, a key it only needs free (`blocked`) included, so a newer job could wait `queued_behind` it for a key it never takes; the daemon keeps the blocked keys with the wait and queues only the others (C-6.9, C-6.11). P3, C-8.4 says an in-place writer's folder is spelled at submit and an allocated writer's is the worktree admission allocates. Review of c7595627: P3, C-8.4's known limit is narrowed (above); P3, with the workdir recorded in its one spelling, `_launch_dir` compared the resume's workdir with its source's worktree, spelled from the state root as given, by `realpath`, so under a state root typed in another case a resume started at its worktree's top instead of its source's place (Review B-2); it compares the one spellings now. Review of abd4c66f: P3, C-8.4 claimed every folder there at submission is recorded in the volume's spelling; a name that cannot be looked up then (a directory above it briefly unsearchable, as well as a tree in quarantine) stays as typed, which the rule and the known limit now say. Detached read-only jobs and allocated writers' source repositories are covered by the detached-job entry below.
- A fenced turn's look prepares no workspace (2026-10-05, measured on 9e159ec9): C-8.4, C-6.10, a turn waiting `lease-held` on retention's fence was looked at again on its backed-off clock (1 s doubling to 30 s) and at once whenever any lease was let go. Each look took the turn's start snapshot (`_workspace`) before the transaction read the fence: in the tree being retired, nine git processes for a writable turn and two for a read-only one, and from the quarantine on, git against a path no longer there. Git moved the times of a nested repository's `.git`, which retention signs, and a git still running when the tree went to quarantine would be a holder there (I7, `retention_holders`). Admission now reads the fence before preparing a turn's workspace (`_fence_hold`: a plain read, then the fence read again inside the transaction that records the wait) and holds a fenced turn as the admitting transaction does: the same hold, naming its folder, the same capacity wait and the same `waiting` row. The transaction's check is kept and still decides; a look whose plain read came just before retention took the fence still prepares the workspace in the tree, and the transaction holds the turn. While the fence is held, the turn's hold is the fence, whatever else it would also wait for (every lane closed, say) or be refused for (its checkout moved to `main`, C-13.2; a route error); the first look after the fence goes finds the rest.
- Admission of a detached job that takes no folder lease, in a tree being retired (2026-10-05, the residual the turn and writer fixes left; probes in the evidence of the turn fix and of this one): C-8.4, C-6.5, C-6.11, a read-only detached job takes no folder lease, and a writer that is not in place takes one only on the worktree the daemon cuts for it, so reservation read no fence for either. Submitted after selection with its workdir in a job's worktree, a folder in it or a repository nested in it, such a job was reserved while retention held the tree's fence (`'live': True`, `'hold': None`), and the quarantine moved the tree from under it before the commit rolled the retirement back (`worktree-in-use`). A writer in a nested repository had worse: preparing its workspace ran `git worktree add` there, registering its worktree inside the tree being archived (N4, `nested-host`). Submit now records such a job's folder, its workdir spelled once (`folder` in `job.submitted`, `folders.canonical`), and admission reads retention's fence on that folder and on every folder above it (`folders.retiring`) twice: before preparing any detached job's workspace (an in-place writer's on its write target), so a look that reads the fence held on that folder runs no git in the tree, and in the reserving transaction, by string operations alone, whose read decides. A retry already holding a worktree the daemon cut is prepared there, under the state root and never inside another job's tree; the writer fix's transaction check covers it. A job queued before the folder was recorded is spelled at each look, outside any transaction, and its fence is read with names folded (`folders.retiring_folded`): spelled while the tree is in quarantine, a workdir typed in another case keeps that case, and an exact read would miss the fence (review of 23a7f1b1, P2). A spelling kept between looks instead (faab6769) lost a quarantine that landed within the look that made it, and hid a workdir replaced since by a symlink; so admission also reads the fence on where any detached job's workdir resolves at the look, beside the folder submit recorded (review of 49651181, P2 twice). The job waits `lease-held`. While the fence is held it is the job's whole hold, whatever else it would also wait for, as for a turn; the first look after the fence goes finds the rest (review of 23a7f1b1, P2: so the early and the transaction's holds agree only on a wait for the fence alone). The hold names the fence as retention's (`retiring`) with the job's folder, and `why` now says retention is removing that tree rather than that another job holds a lease, for turns too (C-6.11).
